# SP7a — Water in the generated world (Design)

Date: 2026-11-17
Status: **Approved by the user on 2026-11-17** ("Vale, doy el visto bueno"), with one addition, recorded in §13.1, §11 and §13.9: the user, approving the spec: "evitar al máximo posible los cortes abruptos de las cascadas, si hay algún caso lo tendré que ver dentro del mundo para saber si rompe mucho la experiencia inmersiva o no". Size: **XL** (about 52 plan tasks; Point 1 gives a trimmed alternative of about 46). This spec turns what the user agreed in the SP7a brainstorm (`.superpowers/sp7a/design-brief.md`) into decisions, values and tests. Items the brief records as the user's words, the user's choices, or a playback the user did not correct are treated as agreed. Every other point is a decision this spec takes (D1-D38), each with an alternative, and none of them is the user's unless it is quoted. Four choices are raised as Points for the user: the size (D38), how many waterfalls (D2), whether caves open under lakes too (D5) and how SP7's old "T still ≤ 10 ms" exit is reconciled with the gates in force (D32).

## Points for the user

The brief leaves every technical and UX choice to this spec (D1-D38, each with its alternative; any of them can be overturned at review). Four choices are the user's: one trades scope against time, one sets how often the thing the user asked for first (waterfalls) appears, one extends the user's own choice about caves under water to lakes, and one changes a performance bar the user approved in the master. Island-edge springs and lava lakes are not Points: the user confirmed both on 2026-11-17 ("Vale, confirmo las dos propuestas"): island-edge springs move to SP7a, lava lakes stay in SP8b. One departure from a playback is this spec's and is stated here so it is seen: the brief's playback capped the settle's horizontal reach at 16 (agreed item 2); D22 caps it at 12 for water and 14 for lava, because the rules read up to 4 (water) or 2 (lava) blocks beyond a front, and only cap + that reach ≤ 16 keeps a stream crossing a chunk border identical from both sides (no seam, the anti-goal "cortes abruptos"). What you see: a stream on a slope runs at most 12 blocks sideways in total instead of 16 before it stops.

**The user's answers** (2026-11-17): Point 1, "Completo, ~52" (the full scope, XL); Point 2, "Voy a ir con unas 50/km2" (about 50 visible waterfalls per km² of steep land, between (a) and (b): one per about 140 × 140 blocks, about 6 in view at RD 12; gated 25-100, half and double the target as the options' bands are); Point 3, "Lagos sellados" (lakes stay sealed); Point 4, "Límites vigentes" (the gates in force plus `aquifer.column`'s own row). The decisions below are written to these answers. The builder check's questions 1-2 (whether settle fronts interact, whether a rule-made source restarts the distance) are answered by §4.1 as fixed after the review; question 4 (A2's denominator) is the controller's call, §13.1.

1. **SP7a's size.** Counted as plan tasks: the first commit, which splits SP7 into SP7a and SP7b (1); the shared fluid rules (1); the aquifer as a pure module and its point function with the barrier-aware solidity probe (3); the aquifer in T replacing water v0, and the wet roof narrowed to lakes (2); the aquifer and spring parameters with U2 (2); the springs: mountain faces, the abyss's lava and island edges (3); the settle in D with its machinery (region reads, the fluid write, the generated-fluid layer), its global reference and the edits reading D's output (4); rule 4's under-water branches with the fast path and the migration of saved worlds (2); the metrics A1-A5, A2, B5's third part, `C7.waterWall` after settle and the re-asserted parts (4); the generator checkpoint, the tuning and the golden record (3); the flowing mesher for water and lava, R1's brute force and R5 (5); Fancy water and the Water setting (2); underwater and lava fog (2); walking in thin water (1); the X-ray's fluid tint and the slice field's aquifer fluid (2); the bench rows and the light row on lava (2); the Chrome legs, the Firefox run and the dry L13 route (3); master pins and the obligations audit (3); review links, screenshots and the smoke (2); the full-tier run, CI at the exit, the exit, the production check and the user's check (5). About 52 tasks: **XL**, beyond the master's L for the whole of SP7 (master :1951; SP6b was XL at 55 and closed at Task 60). **Recommendation:** build it as specified and say XL in SP7a's header (§16 item 1). **Alternative (trimmed, about 46 tasks, "L+"):** (a) Fancy water and the Water setting move to SP11 with the quality presets (Medium stays Fast until then; −2), (b) the X-ray's fluid tint and the slice field's aquifer fluid move to SP10 (flooded caves still show in the X-ray as caves and in the Voxels mode and blocks field as water; −2), (c) walking keeps SP5a's "any water voxel swims" rule (−1), (d) the bench's water segment is dropped and Fancy's 1.2 ms is not measured (−1). Two of these change agreed scope: (a), since Fancy is in the scope the user agreed ("water tiers Fast/Fancy"), and (b), since the agreed split lists "X-ray, map and slice showing flooded caves" and SP6a handed the slice field's aquifer fluid to SP7 (O21). (a) is the only large item that can go without touching the waterfalls, the aquifers or the abyss.

   **What the choice changes:** (a): D10, D28, §7, §13.6's water segment, §16 item 1's contents, §18; (b): D13, §10, §18; (c): D15, §9.2; (d): D36. Both: the size in the header.

2. **How many waterfalls (D2).** The user: "Pienso en ver cascadas naturales cayendo desde puntos altos o montañosos". The anti-goal "Patrones excesivamente predecibles" argues against one on every cliff. A "visible waterfall" here is a water spring in a rock face with its outlet open to the sky and a straight fall of at least 8 blocks (§3.2). Counted per km² of steep land (land positions with `steep` ≥ 1.2 and height ≥ 80, the ground where cliffs are; `surface.cliffSteep`), at RD 12 in all-mountain country (about 0.12 km² in view):
   - **(a) Sparse:** about 25 per km² of steep land (one per 200 × 200 blocks), about 3 in view; gated 12-50.
   - **(b) Moderate (recommended):** about 100 per km² (one per 100 × 100 blocks), about 12 in view; gated 50-200.
   - **(c) Frequent, Minecraft-like:** about 400 per km² (one per 50 × 50 blocks), about 45 in view; gated 200-800.
   The density is set by one leaf (`springs.water.tries`), tuned at D31's checkpoint with screenshots; the user's check judges it. **Recommendation: (b).** **The user chose about 50 per km², gated 25-100.** Island-edge waterfalls (D3) and the abyss's lava falls (D4) have their own counts (§13.1) and are not part of this choice.

   **What the choice changes:** D2, `springs.water.tries`' default (§3.5), `A5.waterfalls`' bounds (§13.1).

3. **Caves under lakes (D5).** The user chose that caves may open under the sea and rivers and flood ("Me parece bien la a"); lakes were not named. **Recommendation: lakes stay sealed.** The wet roof (caves closed within 20 blocks of a water bed, the SP6a spec §1.4) stays for lakes only, so a lake never drains into a cave (B5's third part, "lake water with air horizontally adjacent = 0", master :1518, is then measured as written on T's lake water and gated at 0, §13.2; whether the rim itself holds is measured, not assumed: water v0 left lake-edge walls, O87). Its cost: no flooded cave ever lies under an elevated lake. **Alternative: open under lakes too.** Caves may break through lake beds; the cave fills with lake water down to where barriers or the settle's reach stop it; the wet roof retires completely (the density trees lose its terms, which adds a density-tree migration with its fixture, removes σ_w from the carvers, deletes `caves.limits.waterDepth` with a rewrite of edited trees that reference it, and redefines B5's third part as "lake water spilling sideways into air after settle": about +2-3 tasks).

   **What the choice changes:** D5, D24, §2, §13.2's B5 part, §15.4's migration, the size (+2-3).

4. **SP7's "P1: T still ≤ 10 ms" (D32).** The master's SP7 exit (:1957) asks T to stay ≤ 10 ms p50; it was written before ruling R14 set the gates in force (`terrain.real` ≤ 13 ms p50, ≤ 20 ms p99; `terrain.islands` ≤ 14.5 / 26.5 ms, `test/bench/gates.ts:15-18`), which SP6b kept (its D27) and left to this spec. T is at 9.89 ms p50, 16.92 ms p99 today (master :1678), so 10 ms leaves 0.11 ms, and the aquifer alone is budgeted 1.0 / 2.5 ms (master :1665). **Recommendation: SP7a's exit names the gates in force** (13 / 20 and 14.5 / 26.5 ms) plus the aquifer's own row (`aquifer.column` ≤ 1.0 ms p50, ≤ 2.5 ms p99); the master's "expected 6-9 ms" line stays a target for SP12. **Alternative:** keep 10 ms p50; SP7a then needs a T optimisation work item before the aquifer lands (candidates: a cached non-cave density, fewer cave corners; about +4 tasks, gain unmeasured), and a miss after it goes back to you.

   **What the choice changes:** D32, §13.5, §16 item 1's exit, §19, the size (+4 under the alternative).

## Goal

Water now lives where the terrain puts it. On mountains, here and there a spring flows out of a rock face and falls as a waterfall to the ground below, where it spreads and stops; on floating islands, springs pour from the islands' edges to the ground far below. The waterfalls never run dry: the spring is a permanent source. Seas, rivers and lakes fill at their own levels, and where the coast or a river bank leaves a step, the water flows down it instead of standing as a wall. Underground, caves that open under the sea or a river are flooded, with cracks in the sea floor leading into them, while caves under dry land below sea level are mostly dry, kept dry by rock barriers; deeper down, some caves hold lakes of water or lava, and the deepest gullies end in lava. The abyss generates no water: it is lit only by lava, falling from springs in its walls and pooling on its floor; a stream (a spring's, or a lake's spill) that happens to find a way into it may fall in and spread on its floor (Spec correction, rulings R23). Flowing water and lava show their slope, their falls and the steps between levels. At Medium, water is Fancy: it reflects the sky at grazing angles and ripples. Under water the view closes into a blue fog; in lava, into a short red one. Lush cave floors under water are clay, other cave floors under water gravel. The X-ray tints flooded and lava-filled caves, and the slice view shows aquifer water in caves. Underneath: an aquifer of 16 × 12 × 16 cells with barriers and lava, evaluated per voxel in T; springs placed by T; an exact settle of falls and spreads in D over the 3 × 3 columns, capped at 12 blocks of horizontal travel for water and 14 for lava; a mesher for levels, corner heights and side strips; a water tier uniform; an underwater fog. Measured by A1-A5 (A5 new: springs and falls), A2 after settle, B2's voxel part, B5 with its third part, R5, DT1 with settle, P1 and the Chrome legs. The generated world changes: `GENERATOR_VERSION` 9 → 10 at the one push that changes generated output (D33). The runtime fluid simulation ("break a lake rim and watch it drain") is SP7b.

## Non-goals

- **Fluids that move when you edit:** SP7b (the brief, item 5): the runtime sim (20 TPS, rules, reactions obsidian / cobblestone / stone, caps, sim distance, the diff trailer's ticks), SP5c's paste / cut / move ticks, the ghost drawing fluids, the bench's flood scenario, F1's tick log, F2, F3. In SP7a, breaking a lake rim leaves a still water face, buckets place still sources (SP5a), and a pasted generated waterfall stays frozen.
- **Lava lakes** (Minecraft's surface and underground lava lake feature): SP8b (the user confirmed, 2026-11-17). Deep lava in SP7a comes from aquifer cells, the bottom gullies and the abyss's springs.
- **Vertex waves** on near water: SP11 (master :1958's cut line). **Fancy's animated texture:** it needs SP8a's animation frames, which are cut to SP11 (master :1806); SP7a's Fancy is procedural (D10).
- **Fabulous water** (refraction, absorption): SP11/SP12 (master :1178, :1806).
- **Water in mountain gorges** (rivers above sea level): master §3.4 keeps rivers at 63 and cuts dry gorges on high ground (:616, :619); SP7a keeps that (D8). A spring may feed a gorge; a river running down a mountain is handed to SP12 as a proposal (§17).
- **Ice on water** (freeze_top_layer, rule 5): SP8b (master :822, :893). Cold seas stay liquid in SP7a.
- **Seagrass, kelp, cave decoration in flooded caves:** SP8b. **Water and lava sounds:** SP8c. **Water and lava textures:** SP8a.
- **The full probe at the crosshair, the slice's "aquifer level and status" field and a map aquifer layer:** SP10 (§17); SP7a shows aquifer fluid in the `caves` field and its hover (D13).

## What was agreed earlier, and what is only proposed

From the brief, which records the user's words (quoted in Spanish as the user wrote them) and the items the user saw in a playback and did not correct.
1. **What the user wants to see first** (the user): "Pienso en ver cascadas naturales cayendo desde puntos altos o montañosos, por lo que estos requieren seguramente de una "fuente de agua" para que lo hagan de forma indefinida. Creo que el abismo puede tener lo mismo, pero sumando la lava". The playback, not corrected: waterfalls falling from high or mountainous ground, fed by a source so they never run dry; in the abyss the same with lava.
2. **Springs move forward into SP7a** (the controller's finding and proposal, played back and not corrected): the master gave SP7 only "waterfalls at aquifer borders" and put springs in SP8b; water springs in exposed rock faces on slopes and mountains (Minecraft's spring rule: a source in a stone wall with exactly one face to air) and lava springs in the abyss come into SP7a in a minimal form, feeding the gen-time settle (falling unlimited, horizontal reach capped at 16; this spec departs from the 16, see the Points preamble and D22); the source is permanent, so the waterfall stays; the rest of decoration (trees, ores, crystals) stays SP8b.
3. **The abyss: lava only** (the user): "Pensé por un momento en añadirlas al abismo, pero me gusta la idea de que el abismo sea un lugar inhóspito, así que dejamos solo la lava finalmente". No water in the abyss; lava falls and lava lighting it a little (SP6b's hand-over, master :1956, with the user's SP6b words "y la propia lava que también emite luz"). Amended by the user on 2026-11-23 (rulings R23): "dejaremos que el agua caiga hacia el abismo (no es que el abismo genere agua, si no que la generación del terreno y los manantiales pueden dar la coincidencia de generarse de tal forma que aparezcan casos así)": the abyss still generates no water, but water a stream or a spill brings to its air falls into it (D4).
4. **Caves under the sea and rivers** (the user chose option (a): "Me parece bien la a"): caves may open under the sea and rivers, and those beneath fill with water (cracks in the sea floor into flooded caves, clay or gravel floors); aquifer barriers keep nearby dry caves dry; this retires SP6a's wet roof and decides `caves.limits.waterDepth`. (The brief's wording. Lakes were not named; this spec recommends keeping the roof under lakes only, which is Point 3 and D24, not part of this agreed item.)
5. **The split** (the user: "Estoy de acuerdo con la forma 1"): SP7 becomes SP7a, the generated world's water (aquifers with dry, flooded and under-sea caves and barriers; rivers and lakes at their own level; deep lava and the abyss's lava; water springs on slopes and lava springs in the abyss with the gen-time settle; the flowing water and lava mesher; water tiers Fast and Fancy; underwater fog and tint; clay and gravel under cave water; X-ray, map and slice showing flooded caves; A1-A4, B2 voxel, B5, F1-F3 as they apply to generation, R5, DT1 with settle, P1), and SP7b, fluids that move when you edit (the runtime sim, its rules, reactions, caps, sim distance and diffs; SP5c's paste, cut and move ticks; the ghost drawing fluids; the bench's flood scenario; "break a lake rim and watch it drain"). The reason given to the user: SP7a delivers the waterfalls in the first merge; the sim is separate and the riskiest part.
6. **Island-edge springs in SP7a and lava lakes in SP8b** (the user, 2026-11-17: "Vale, confirmo las dos propuestas"): waterfalls from floating islands' edges fit "cascadas desde puntos altos".
7. **Earlier words** (the user, SP6a and SP6b): "sería imprescindible la presencia de minerales y de vez en cuando tener acuíferos y fuentes o lagos de lava" (the SP6a spec :57) and "Me parece bien que lleguen en SP7 y SP8" (the SP6b spec :62): aquifers and springs arrive here, lava lakes and ores in SP8b.
8. **Anti-goals** (the user's words, carried from SP6): "Patrones excesivamente predecibles" and "encontrar muchos cortes abruptos en la generación". For water this spec reads them as: no waterfall on every cliff and no grid of them; no water standing as a vertical wall where it should flow; no seam where a stream crosses a chunk border.
9. **Proposals the brief lists and this spec keeps as proposals:** vertex waves stay cut to SP11 (above); the reconciliation of "P1: T still ≤ 10 ms" (Point 4, now settled by the user: the gates in force); the CI budget (≤ 6 min 0 s on cold runs, §14).
10. **Process** (as SP6a and SP6b): spec with reviews and a builder check, Points for the user, approval, plan, subagent-driven execution, merge after the user's go-ahead, production check (Chrome by the agent under the GPU conditions, Firefox by the user), the user's check, "docs: SP7a complete".

Everything below that is not one of these items is this spec's proposal.

## Decisions taken by this spec (for the user's review)

### Decisions with a visible effect

- **D1 Waterfalls from springs in mountain faces** (§3.2). *You notice:* on steep ground, a stream comes out of a rock face one block wide, falls straight down at least `springs.water.minFall` (7) blocks onto the ground below (8 until Task 28a; §13.1 counts a fall as visible from 8) and spreads there into a small pool-shaped spill that thins out over about 7 blocks; it never runs dry. The spring sits in natural rock (stone, deepslate, dirt, calcite, terracotta and the like; never sand or gravel), with rock above, below and on three sides and its one open side facing the drop, as Minecraft's spring. Springs appear only where a face drops at least `springs.water.minFall` (7) blocks below them and is open to the sky, so they land on mountains and cliffs, not on flat land or in caves. Alternative: Minecraft's uniform tries at any height (about 25 per chunk, **MC (memory)**), which put most springs in cave walls and few on mountains.
- **D2 How many waterfalls** (Point 2). *You notice:* with the user's choice (about 50 per km²), about one waterfall per 140 × 140 blocks of steep land, about 6 in view in all-mountain country at RD 12; positions look scattered (no rows, no spacing pattern; `A5.clarkEvans` and `A5.borderRatio`, §13.1). Alternative: Point 2's (a), (b) or (c).
- **D3 Island-edge waterfalls** (§3.3; agreed). *You notice:* in floating_islands, now and then a spring pours from an island's side and falls to the ground or sea below, a long thin waterfall; about 5 per km² of island area (**estimate**, tuned at D31). Alternative: none (SP8b, as the master had it).
- **D4 The abyss: lava only** (§1.6, §3.4). *You notice:* the abyss generates no water: no aquifer water there; a stream or a lake's spill that reaches abyss air falls into it and spreads on its floor or meets its lava under the settle's ordinary rules (rare: it needs a spring stream to find a shaft into the abyss; Spec correction, rulings R23: the user withdrew the earlier "a stream that falls into it from above stops at its edge", which left streams cut at the abyss's edge, Task 27m's shots). Lava comes from three places: lava springs in the abyss's walls (lava falls of at least 4 blocks that spread about 3 blocks where they land), lava pools from aquifer lava cells where the abyss lies below y −10, and the gullies of the rock floor (D7). Each lava voxel lights its surroundings (D14), so the abyss is dark except near lava. Alternative: SP6b's other suggestion, a lava mask lowering the rock floor locally so wide lava lakes fill the abyss's bottom (more lava, moves C7's bottom parts, D7's alternative).
- **D5 Caves open under the sea and rivers; lakes stay sealed** (§2; agreed for sea and rivers, Point 3 for lakes). *You notice:* the sea floor and river beds have cracks and holes into caves, which are full of water; flooded caves under the sea are common, as in Minecraft, because cells under the ocean flood more readily (§1.3); elevated lakes keep a sealed bed (no cave within 20 blocks below them). Alternative: Point 3's.
- **D6 Dry caves below sea level** (§1). *You notice:* under land, caves below y 63 are mostly dry; here and there a cave holds a lake at its own level (a partially flooded cell, levels at varied heights); where a flooded and a dry part meet, a rock wall (a barrier) usually separates them; where none does, water spills out and falls into the dry part (an underground waterfall: the master's "waterfalls at aquifer borders"). A1 holds ≥ 40 % of such cave air dry. Alternative: Minecraft's exact thresholds without the 2D `offset` inputs (D18), which needs the 3D surface estimate.
- **D7 Deep lava** (§1.4). *You notice:* below y −10, some cave lakes are lava instead of water (aquifer lava cells, levels from about −29 to −11, and from −55 to −51 in the deepest cells); and the lowest voxels of the rock floor's gullies (y −52 and −51) hold lava wherever no cell claims them (master :756's "everything below y −54 is lava", with the line raised to `aquifer.lavaLevel` −50, the user's choice at D31, rulings R23: at −54 it filled nothing, since no cave air lies below −52; a 1-2-block lava sheet at the bottom of the deepest gullies, Task 27m's shots). No floor change: SP6b's rock floor, its mounds and gullies stay, and no bedrock shows. Alternative: D4's lava mask lowering the floor, or a raised global lava line (e.g. −50), which fills 4-5 blocks of every gully with a flat lava plane at one height (reads as a flat floor again, the thing the user disliked at the world's bottom in SP6a).
- **D8 Seas, rivers and lakes at their own level, and their walls flow** (§1.7, §4). *You notice:* seas and rivers at 63, lakes at their level, as today; where water v0 left a wall of water against air (a river bank whose position is dry while the next is wet, an archipelago's sea above a lake below sea level, master :1955; the SP3e spec :190), the water now flows down over it as a short spill. Mountain gorges stay dry unless a spring feeds them. Alternative: also raise rivers to their own height in gorges (§17, a proposal for SP12), which changes the column stage and the map.
- **D9 Flowing water and lava look** (§6). *You notice:* a spreading spill slopes down from its source, each block a ninth lower; where two levels meet, the step shows a thin side strip instead of a hole; a falling stream is a full-height column with straight sides; lava flows the same way (shorter, 3 blocks); still lava's surface now sits at 8/9 of a block like water's (it was a full block). Alternative: lava sources at full height (SP5a's look), which leaves a 1/9 step where still lava meets flowing lava.
- **D10 Fancy water at Medium** (§7). *You notice:* water reflects the sky more at grazing angles (Fresnel) and shows two layers of small moving ripples; the deep-to-shallow colour stays. Settings › Graphics gains "Water: Fast / Fancy", live, Fancy by default (master §4.16: Medium is Fancy, :1261). Alternative: Fast stays the default until SP11's quality presets (Point 1 (a)).
- **D11 Underwater and in lava** (§8). *You notice:* when the eye is below the water's surface, the view closes into a blue fog (fully fogged at about 56 blocks, denser in deep or dark water) with a blue tint, the sky seen through the surface included; in lava, an orange-red fog fully closed at about 3 blocks. Leaving the water returns the normal fog in the same frame. The X-ray view is unaffected. Alternative: fog only (no tint), which leaves the sky dome untinted under water.
- **D12 Cave floors under water** (§5). *You notice:* the floor of a flooded lush cave is clay (two blocks deep) instead of moss; the floor of any other flooded cave is gravel (one block); the abyss holds no aquifer water (a stream the settle brings into it is D's water, rulings R23). Floors under settle's spills (D's water) keep their dry skin (moss, stone), since the rules run in T. Alternative: no under-water branch (moss under water).
- **D13 Seeing flooded caves** (§10). *You notice:* in the X-ray, the walls of flooded caves are tinted toward blue and those of lava-filled caves toward orange, in both colour modes, so flooded caves stand out from dry ones (the legend gains "Flooded" and "Lava"); the Density tab's "Cave type" slice field draws aquifer water in caves (blue) and lava (orange), and its hover adds "water (aquifer level 63, flooded)" or "dry (aquifer empty)"; the slice legend lists lava apart from water (today it counts lava as water, `src/ui/inspector/sliceModel.ts:457`). Alternative: the X-ray unchanged (flooded caves already draw as caves, recon-render §11), which cannot tell a flooded cave from a dry one.
- **D14 Lava lights as in Minecraft** (§9.1). *You notice:* every lava voxel emits light 15, so the ground within about 14 blocks of lava is lit warm; the abyss is lit only near its lava, so it stays mostly dark, lit by "la propia lava que también emite luz" (the user, the SP6b spec :58). Alternative: a lower lava emission parameter for a dimmer abyss (touches L1's reference and every lava light, recon-render §5).
- **D15 Walking through thin water** (§9.2). *You notice:* you swim only when the fluid's surface is more than a wade depth (a quarter of a block) above your feet, so walking through the thin edge of a spill (a level-7 film, 1/9 of a block) no longer slows you as deep water does; falling streams still slow a fall (Spec correction 15: a body on the ground has its feet at the film voxel's y + 0, below any surface height above 0, so "below the surface height" alone would still swim in a film). Alternative: SP5a's rule (any water voxel the body overlaps swims, `src/player/walk.ts:201-204`).
- **D16 Finding everything for the review** (§13.9). *You notice:* before the user's check, the controller hands over links the harness found: three mountain waterfalls (the tallest visible falls near spawn), an island waterfall, a lava fall and a lava pool in the abyss, a flooded cave under the sea (opened with the X-ray on), a dry cave below sea level, a river mouth and a lake, a coast for Fancy water and an underwater pose. Alternative: flying with the map only.

### Technical decisions (no visible effect)

- **D17 The aquifer runs in T, per tagged non-solid voxel, before the surface pass** (§1.7). Order: density → tops → carve → tag and cave biome → **aquifer** (fluid and barriers) → surface biomes → surface pass (reading the aquifer's water, so rule 4 sees cave water) → **springs** → **seed sections** (§1.7) → sections → aux A and B. The tag and the nibble are written before the aquifer and kept on water voxels (O31); a barrier voxel becomes solid and loses its tag. It is the master's "each carved voxel asks `aquifer.fluidAt`" (:742) generalised: every tagged non-solid voxel (cave air, carved or a cave noise's, tag kinds 1-9, `src/gen/caves/tags.ts:300`) asks it. The terrain's own air (untagged: sky, overhangs) never does (D19). Alternative: the aquifer in D (no rule-4 branches, since rules run in T; the slice could not show it from the proto).
- **D18 Cell levels read the 2D `offset` and `surfaceWaterLevel` at the cell's grid column** (§1.2). The near-surface rule ("within 12 blocks of `surfaceEst`", master :751) is evaluated at each cell's un-jittered grid column (x, z), through `samplePoint` (`src/gen/column/columnPoint.ts:72`), 9 grid columns per T column (**estimate** 0.07 ms), pure and identical in every column. No `surfaceEst3` and no quart-lattice cache (master :716's first consumer is not this one; O24 → SP9). The level-field −∞ rule (SP6a D19) is not needed: the aquifer never reads `col` (O23). Alternative: `surfaceEst3` at the 9 grid columns (4-8 tap probes each, unmeasured), which follows overhangs but adds cost for no rule that needs it.
- **D19 Untagged air by the position's level; cave air by cells** (§1.7). An untagged non-solid voxel (the terrain's own air: the sky, overhangs, the gap under a floating island) takes water iff y ≤ ⌊`surfaceWaterLevel`⌋ at the position (water v0's level read, `src/gen/pipeline/terrainStage.ts:148`); a tagged non-solid voxel asks the aquifer (D17). Both rules are point functions of (x, y, z): neither reads the column's `top`, so `fluidAt`, `barrierAt` and `terrainSolidAt` (D20) are exact at any voxel, including the open sky near a coast. Oceans, rivers and lakes fill as today. This spec calls the first rule's water **sky-open water** (an overhang's air below the level takes it too). Water v0's 12-block rule (`WATER_DEPTH`, `:86`, `:152-157`) and its island base (`BASE`, `:107`, `:160-177`) retire: the base only bounded that depth. An untagged pocket deep under the sea or a river now fills to the level (rare; D31 records the count of sky-open water voxels more than 12 below `top`). Aux A's WORLD_SURFACE_WG no longer rests on a proof (`terrainStage.ts:288-292`): a tagged voxel above `top` can hold aquifer water (a crack in the sea floor), so the aquifer step tracks the highest fluid voxel per position and WORLD_SURFACE_WG = max(`top`, that voxel) + 1. Alternative: the aquifer at every non-solid voxel at or below the column's highest solid voxel (this draft's first form). It needs `top`, which the point functions cannot know without a downward probe scan (about 200 density evaluations per position), so `terrainSolidAt` would disagree with T in the open sky near coasts (`DT2.probeBulk`'s near-top stratum, `test/metrics/region.metric.ts:209-212`).
- **D20 Barriers are rock, and the probe knows them** (§1.5). A barrier voxel is solid (its state from the surface pass: stone, deepslate or rule 4's skin), its tag cleared. Barriers form only on tagged voxels (D17). `terrainSolidAt(inp, dc, x, y, z)` (`src/gen/fluids/solidAt.ts`) = (the density probe's `solidAt`: its solidity and not carved) or (`caveTagAt` ≠ 0 and `barrierAt`), a pure point function that reads no column property. The density probe's `solidAt` (`src/gen/density/probe.ts:36`, SP6a D22) keeps its name and meaning (Spec correction 4); every caller that compares a probe with T's stored solidity moves to `terrainSolidAt` in the commit where T first writes barriers (the plan's Task 13): DT2's `probeBulk` (`test/harness/dt2.ts`; K8, Q15), `test/unit/presetTrees.test.ts:326`, `test/unit/terrainStage.test.ts:116`, `test/unit/floatingIslands.test.ts:212`, `test/unit/caveTags.test.ts:388` and the smoke's pose corners (`test/unit/uiSmoke.test.ts`). Alternative: barriers excluded from `probeBulk` (a part that no longer checks every solid voxel).
- **D21 Springs are placed by T by pure rules, with point probes at chunk borders** (§3). Each spring's column decides it from a per-chunk RNG, its own column's solidity, states, tags and heights, and, for the few neighbour voxels outside the column, the exact point functions (`terrainSolidAt`, `fluidAt`, `caveTagAt` with `caveBiomeAt`; DT2 proves them equal to bulk). Every try reads the world before any spring (a spring never sees another, in its own column or a neighbour's), so the decision is the same from any column. No border ring is excluded. Springs enter D as proto sources, which is exactly settle's input (master :921). Their leaves are Terrain-scope (a change re-runs T, as any terrain leaf). Alternative: master §3.12's D step 1 (a placed-feature step), which needs SP8b's decorate-scope Apply path and either a spring-free ring at every chunk border (a grid artifact, recon-gen §13) or the same probes.
- **D22 The settle in D: exact, with caps 12 and 14** (§4). Rules in a shared `src/world/blocks/fluidRules.ts` (where master §1 places it, :104-105; gen, mesh, sim, player and ui may all import `world/`; SP7b's sim reuses them): falling unlimited; spread one level per block, reaching 7 blocks (water) and 3 blocks (lava, levels 0, 2, 4, 6, **MC (memory)**: lava drops 2 per block in the overworld's 8-step scale); flow only toward the nearest drop within 4 (water) or 2 (lava), all open sides when none, by a search that never enters, nor takes as a first side, a voxel holding a proto source of the fluid's own type (Minecraft's slope search, **MC (memory)**; Spec correction, rulings R21, Task 25's finding: otherwise a lake's or the sea's own voxels over water are drops at distance 1 and a shore never spills down a bank or coast step); a new water source between ≥ 2 horizontal water sources of the proto over solid or a proto water source. Every read of fluid that decides a rule reads the **proto** (T's output, inside the window), never another front's result; each voxel's horizontal distance is the least over the feeders that give it its level (one least distance per level, so a voxel's level and its distance come from one derivation and the cap counts along the path that carried the level), and a new source inherits its feeders' distance (+1) and never restarts it. The rules are then monotone, the settled world is their least fixed point, and it does not depend on processing order. Each front stops at **12 (water) and 14 (lava)**: the nearest-drop search reads up to 4 (2) blocks beyond a front, so every read that decides a voxel of K lies within cap + search ≤ 16 blocks of it, which is what makes a 3 × 3 result equal to a global settle on K (recon-gen §7's caveat; master :929's claim, restated over every read). A truncated front keeps `unsettled`. Every non-AIR state's `FLUID_MODE` is read from the table (`src/world/blocks/kinds.ts:62-67`): `block` stops fluid, `hold` takes the byte, `displace` becomes AIR plus fluid (no generated state is `hold` or `displace` before SP8a/SP8b, so this costs nothing now and nothing later, O39). Fluid types never mix: water and lava settle as two independent fixed points, each treating the other type's proto fluid as `block`, merged per voxel with lava winning and the merged voxel marked `unsettled` (a contact SP7b's reactions resolve; reactions are SP7b's). A water front enters abyss air as any other (D4, §4.1; Spec correction, rulings R23: the earlier stop at the abyss's nibble is withdrawn, and no rule reads the nibble). Alternative: cap 16 with the search clamped to the window (the brief's 16), which makes two neighbours compute a stream crossing their border differently: a seam where streams bend or stop at chunk borders.
- **D23 The minimal D machinery** (§4.2, §9.3). D reads the 3 × 3 proto through its section views (no 48 × 384 × 48 copy; cost follows the visits), writes K's settled fluid copy-on-write (a `replaceFluid` beside `replaceBlocks`, `src/world/store/api.ts:96-101`; `replaceBlocks` already takes fluid, :101, but would move the blocks channel to a new slot too, so `replaceFluid` exists to keep the blocks slot shared with the proto), and keeps a **generated-fluid layer**: for each section whose fluid settle changed, the settled fluid before the diff overlay (a reference to the final fluid slot when the column has no diff, so the layer costs a slot of its own only where a diff rewrote a settled section), so the overlay rebuild (`src/gen/pipeline/decorateStage.ts:59-82`) and the edit path's "generated value" (`src/sim/edits.ts:315`) read D's output, not the proto (master :1975's SP5a → SP8b hand-over, triggered by settle in SP7a). SP8b extends the layer to blocks. Alternative: recompute settle on every edit's generated-value read (a 3 × 3 settle per edit on the sim worker).
- **D24 The wet roof narrows to lakes** (§2). `wetMask` (`src/gen/column/columnPoint.ts:58-64`, `:87`, `:113`, and the column stage's own `WET_MASK(surfaceWaterLevel)`, `src/gen/column/columnStage.ts:30`, `:107`) becomes 1 where the position's **lake** level is finite (inside a lake mask) instead of where any `surfaceWaterLevel` is; the density trees' wet terms (`src/core/params/defaultTrees.ts:73-81`, `:126`, `:140-142`) and the carvers' σ_w (`src/gen/caves/carve.ts:13-24`, `:116-120`) are unchanged and now act under lakes only. `caves.limits.waterDepth` (16) stays a live leaf, the lake roof's depth. No density tree changes, so no density-tree migration; `wetMask` stays out of the SP2a digest (`src/metrics/sp2aGoldens.ts:43-47`) and `pointDigest` (`src/metrics/pointDigest.ts:27-28`), so `sp2a.*` and `sp3e.column.point.*` stay unchanged. An edited tree reading `col wetMask` now reads the lake mask (stated in the schema's help text); `caves.limits.waterDepth`'s help ("under lakes, rivers and seas", `src/core/params/schema.ts:302`) becomes "under lakes". The column sample's `wetMask` changes, so the stage that owns it bumps (`shape` 2 → 3, `src/core/stage/registry.ts:18`, which computes it with the lake fields; the plan confirms the owner, §15.3). Alternative: Point 3's (the terms leave the trees, the leaf is deleted).
- **D25 Rule 4's under-water branches, on the fast path** (§5). Two leaves in rule 4 of both built-in surface trees, before the lush and default floor branches: under cave water, lush floors clay two deep, other floors gravel one deep. The fast path gains a water-gated class: a run with no water above its top takes the band state without evaluation, as today; only runs with `waterAbove` evaluate their top voxels (`src/gen/surface/compile.ts:28-31`, `:260`, `:456-458`), so SP6b's biome-gated skip stays (recon-gen §9). `rulesCost` learns the class. Alternative: an ungated "gravel elsewhere" leaf, which turns SP6b's skip off and costs about +0.2-0.3 ms of T (the SP6b spec :272).
- **D26 The flowing mesher** (§6): Minecraft's corner average (**MC (memory)**) rounded to ninths; side strips between unequal levels of one fluid (amends master :1060 and R1's brute force, `test/harness/refs.ts:125-126`, `:155`, in lockstep); a fixed split diagonal; lava in the opaque pass with its four corner drops in the solid word's `flags` nibble when `layer` is `LAVA_LAYER` (recon-render §2 option 2: no new pass, no new draw call). Alternative: a lava pass of its own with the fluid word (pass 4: +1 batch and draw per region with lava, recon-render §2 option 1).
- **D27 R5 counts surface quads, and its gap is closed by measure, then levers** (§6.5). R5 reads "water quads on surface up faces over distinct surface up faces" (master :1552's "surface quads"), on named water regions per tier. The definition is set here, from the start (it is not a lever). A measuring task first reads today's 0.2236 (`test/metrics/.out/task38/full/R5.json`, a local file) under it and splits it by face and by merge key; then the lever: `depth` quantised to 4 bands in the top face's key. The second lever once listed here (light per vertex out of key B for uniform-light still tops) is dropped (Spec correction 16): uniform-light tops with equal light already merge under today's key B; merging unequal lights would mis-light the quad. A further lever must name a key change that merges faces whose key-B words differ, with its visible cost judged in a shot, and goes to the user. Sloped quads are in the gated ratio (one per voxel, so they weigh against it) and also recorded apart (`R5.slopedShare`). Alternative: keep counting all faces and bring sides and down faces under 0.1 too (no lever is known for them).
- **D28 Fancy is a uniform, not a program** (§7). One water program; a `waterTier` uniform branches (as `xrayColorMode`, `src/render/materials/index.ts:64-68`); a `timeSeconds` uniform drives the ripples; sloped surfaces take their normal from `dFdx`/`dFdy` of the world position. No relink on toggle (a link costs about 170 ms cold on the Iris Xe, `src/render/renderer.ts:413-418`). Alternative: a `#define` per tier (two programs linked at load).
- **D29 The eye's fluid by its surface height** (§8). The frame step reads the eye voxel's fluid byte (a new read, in the pattern of `eyeSkyLight`, `src/ui/world/frameStep.ts:141-147`, which reads sky light; the store already exposes `fluidAt` to the frame step, `:97`) and compares the eye's height in the voxel with the fluid's surface height (§6.1's height: 8/9 for a source, (8 − level)/9 flowing, 1 when falling or with the same fluid above); `FrameState` gains `eyeFluid` (0, 1 water, 2 lava). Alternative: the eye voxel's fluid type alone, which fogs an eye just above a source's surface.
- **D30 The metrics: A1-A4, a new A5, and settled readings over the caves regions** (§13). A global settle reference (`test/harness/refs.ts`) runs over each caves-metric region's T dump, so A1-A5, A2 and `C7.waterWall` read the D-equivalent world without generating D (B5's third part reads T, §13.2); DT1's new parts prove the windowed settle equals the reference (SP6a's `carverReference` pattern). A5 (springs and falls) is a new id with a master row. Alternative: generate D on the regions (about 3 × the T cost), or put the spring parts under A4 (an id that counts water bodies).
- **D31 A generator checkpoint before the tuning** (§13.8): after the aquifer, the springs, the settle and the metrics exist locally and before the tuning and the golden record: A1-A5, A2, `C7.waterWall`, `terrain.real`, `aquifer.column`, the D row's visits, B3's minima, the waterfall screenshots; with a rule and levers in order. Alternative: misses found at the exit.
- **D32 P1** (§13.5; Point 4): the gates in force, `aquifer.column` ≤ 1.0 / 2.5 ms gated, `decorate.column` (with settle) recorded against master's 3 / 8 ms (gated in SP8b, master :1978), `light.column.lava` gated at `light.column`'s 4 / 8 ms, `mesh.section` re-measured on flowing water. Alternative: Point 4's 10 ms.
- **D33 `GENERATOR_VERSION` 9 → 10: one output-changing push** (§15.3), as SP6b's D28. The aquifer, the wet roof's narrowing, the springs, rule 4's branches and the tuning change generated output and land in one push recorded from its base; settle changes no golden of today (region goldens are T-only), but its new golden lands in the same push. Alternative: two pushes (aquifer, then springs; 11 at the exit).
- **D34 Saved worlds move to SP7a's rules** (§15.4): `SCHEMA_VERSION` 5 → 6, `MIGRATIONS[4]` maps a stored built-in surface tree (default, floating_islands) at `GENERATOR_VERSION` 9 to SP7a's; edited trees are kept; the new `aquifer.*` and `springs.*` leaves take the profile's values. Alternative: no migration (worlds saved in SP6b get no clay or gravel under cave water).
- **D35 CI ≤ 6 min 0 s on cold runs, 20 parallel jobs** (§14), with the new files named into shards by measured time, never into the catch-alls. Alternative: a 21st job (past the Free plan's 20).
- **D36 Every Chrome route re-measured into `assets/sp7a/`** (§13.6), with L13 on a dry route and a water segment (Fast against Fancy at a coast) for master §7's "water (Fancy) ≤ 1.2 ms". Alternative: only the stream and a water segment; other cells read from `assets/sp6b/`.
- **D37 Items handed on rather than taken** (§17): the current push and drains → SP7b; the crosshair probe's aquifer lines, the slice's aquifer level and status field, the map's aquifer layer and F3's fluid line → SP10; RegionMesh's per-section translucent sort → SP11 if the Firefox look needs it (§6.6); elevated rivers → SP12 (proposal); `surfaceEst3` caching → SP9. Alternative per item in §17.
- **D38 SP7a is XL** (Point 1). Alternative: Point 1's trimmed scope.

## Module layout after SP7a

```
src/world/blocks/fluidRules.ts           the shared rules: levels, falling, spread, nearest drop, new sources, caps; FLUID_MODE handling;
                                         the surface height of §6.1 (read by gen, mesh, player, ui; master §1, :104-105) (§4.1, §6.1)
src/gen/fluids/aquifer.ts                cells, statuses, levels, lava, barriers, the abyss, the fast path; fluidAt, barrierAt (§1)
src/gen/fluids/springs.ts                the three spring kinds and their per-chunk tries (§3)
src/gen/fluids/settle.ts                 the 3 × 3 settle over section views; seeds, fronts, unsettled (§4)
src/gen/fluids/solidAt.ts                terrainSolidAt, the barrier-aware point solidity (D20; the density probe's solidAt keeps its name)

src/gen/pipeline/decorateStage.ts        settle at D's step 2; the generated-fluid layer; the overlay from settled output (§4.2)
src/gen/column/columnPoint.ts columnStage.ts   wetMask from the lake level (D24)
src/core/stage/registry.ts               terrain's params gain 'aquifer' and 'springs'; versions (§15.3)

src/world/store/api.ts store.ts section.ts aux.ts   replaceFluid; the generated-fluid layer (D23); aux B's seedSections (§1.7)
src/sim/edits.ts                         generatedAt reads the generated-fluid layer (§9.3)
src/mesh/fluid.ts packing.ts greedy.ts   corner heights, side strips, falls, lava drops in the flags nibble (§6)
src/mesh/xray.ts                         the fluid bits of the xray word (§10.1)
src/render/materials/glsl/water.glsl.ts terrain.glsl.ts common.glsl.ts sky.glsl.ts xray.glsl.ts   Fancy, lava drops, underwater fog and tint, the X-ray fluid tint (§6-§8, §10)

src/ui/world/frameStep.ts settings.ts settingsPanel.ts teleport.ts   the eye's fluid; the Water row (§7, §8)
src/player/walk.ts                       the fluid-height rule (§9.2)

test/harness/refs.ts                     the global settle reference (§13.4)
test/harness/caveMetrics.ts water.ts     A1-A5, A2, C7.waterWall after settle, B5's third part (§13.1-§13.2)
```
