
## 1. Aquifers (D5-D7, D17-D20; master §3.9)

### 1.1 Cells

As master :748: cells 16 × 12 × 16 blocks with the grid offset (5, 1, 5); each cell's centre jittered by (0-9, 0-8, 0-9) from h = `hash3(deriveSeed(seed, 'aquifer.cell'), i, j, k) >>> 0` (the cell's indices; `hash4` is a salted 2D hash, `src/core/hash.ts:36`, so the jitter is `hash3`, `:32`, as the cave biome's), x + h mod 10, y + (h >>> 8) mod 9, z + (h >>> 16) mod 10 from the cell's corner (16i, 12j, 16k); each voxel takes the 4 nearest centres among the 2 × 3 × 2 neighbourhood (squared distances, ties to the lower cell index; master :748's 4, where Minecraft keeps 3, **MC (memory)**; only the two nearest decide here, so the count is harmless). A cell's status is (level, fluid type): level an integer or −∞ (empty), type water or lava.

### 1.2 Levels near the surface (D18)

For a cell with grid column (gx, gz) (its un-jittered x, z) and centre y: `p = samplePoint(gx, gz)`. If `centre.y ≥ p.offset − aquifer.surfaceBand` (12), the cell takes ⌊`p.surfaceWaterLevel`⌋: 63 under sea or river, Lw under a lake, −∞ on land. So near the surface, caves under the sea and rivers flood to 63, caves under land stay empty, and no near-surface cell floods dry land (a reading of master :751 that also covers the land case).

### 1.3 Floodedness and partial levels

Otherwise, Nf = `aquifer.noises.floodedness` (3D) at the centre, clamped to [−1, 1]; the ocean factor t = clamp((63 − p.offset)/8, 0, 1); thresholds lerp(inland, ocean, t): **fully flooded** if Nf > lerp(0.8, −0.3, t) (level 63, water); **partially flooded** if Nf > lerp(0.4, −0.8, t), level = ⌊centre.y/40⌋·40 + 20 + 3·round(Nspread·10/3) (Nspread = `aquifer.noises.spread` at the centre, clamped to [−1, 1]; levels −9 … +9 around each 40-block band's middle); otherwise **empty** (master :752-755). Under the sea (t = 1) most deep cells flood; inland few do. The thresholds are leaves (§1.9).

### 1.4 Lava (D7)

A partially flooded cell whose level ≤ `aquifer.lavaMaxLevel` (−10) and |Nlava| > `aquifer.lavaThreshold` (0.3), Nlava = `aquifer.noises.lava` sampled once per 64 × 40 × 64 block (at the block's corner), is lava (master :756). Below the master's line, any tagged non-solid voxel at y < `aquifer.lavaLevel` (−50) that no cell fills is lava (a source; master's "everything below y −54 is lava", its line raised): under SP6b's rock floor the deepest tagged air lies at y −52 (Task 27g's and Task 27m's readings), so this fills y −52 and −51 in the deepest gullies (Spec correction, rulings R23 item 5: the level was −54, and the text said it filled y −55, where no cave air lies, so it filled nothing). Expected lava cave lakes: levels about −29 … −11 (cells centred −40 … 0) and −55 … −51 (cells centred below −40). **Why not the floor mask** (SP6b's hand-over, master :1956): it moves `C7.bottomPlane`, `bottomFloorSpread`, `bottomFlatRatio` and `bedrockExposed` and adds a noise and a leaf, for a lava plane at the world's bottom that this spec does not need: A3's first part (lava below −54 in ≥ 50 % of 32 × 32-chunk regions; since D31 the share of the tagged voxels at y < `aquifer.lavaLevel` holding lava, §13.1, rulings R21, R23) is met by the floor rule's lava at −52 … −51 (Task 27n: 78-95 % per group; at −54 its population was empty), the floor mask staying the user's alternative. `C7.bedrockExposed` is re-checked on every group (its population, cave air with no fluid, never touches lava's bedrock neighbours: the rock floor keeps air ≥ −55 and bedrock ≤ −60; at −50 it reads 0 on every group, Task 27n).

### 1.5 Barriers (D20)

At a tagged non-solid voxel (D17), when its two nearest centres have different statuses and d2 − d1 < `aquifer.barrierBand` (30, squared-distance units; master :758's 25, raised at D31 by Task 27l, the user's choice, rulings R21), Minecraft's pressure formula (**MC (memory)**: a similarity 1 − (d2 − d1)/`barrierBand` times a pressure from the two levels and the voxel's height, plus `aquifer.noises.barrier` × 2) decides; a positive result places a barrier: the voxel becomes solid, its tag kind and nibble 0, its state chosen by the surface pass (stone, deepslate, rule 4's skin or the abyss's strata). Otherwise the voxel takes the nearest centre's status: fluid if y ≤ level, else air, for water and lava alike: this project's levels are the highest fluid y (water v0's `y ≤ waterTop`, D19's sky-open rule `y ≤ ⌊surfaceWaterLevel⌋`), and Minecraft's `at(y)` (fluid at y < level) is the same rule with its level one higher (Spec correction 7; otherwise a crack under the sea would hold a dry layer at y 63 between the cave water and the sea). Two statuses hold fluid at y, for the pressure's water-against-lava case, by the same rule.

### 1.6 The abyss (D4)

Two tests per tagged voxel, both point functions:
- **Inside the abyss:** the voxel's own cave nibble (written before the aquifer, D17) is the abyss (3). The nibble is picked at a position jittered by up to `caves.biomes.edgeJitter` (2) blocks in x, y and z (`caveBiomeOfPass`, `src/gen/biomes/cavePicker.ts:183-186`; `src/core/params/schema.ts:365`), so it is tested directly, not through the unjittered box. There a water status yields **air**: the aquifer never writes water to an abyss voxel. Lava statuses and the floor rule (`aquifer.lavaLevel`, −50) apply unchanged.
- **The margin:** a voxel whose nibble is not the abyss but which lies inside the abyss box widened on every side, from the cave-biome column pass's unjittered C′, E′ and Y0 (`cavePicker.ts:120-151`; the point function reads them through `caveBiomeUnjittered`, `:189-191`): E′ ≤ `abyss.eMax` + m, C′ > `abyss.cMin` − m and depth > `abyss.depthMin` − 0.1 (master :760's 0.1 depth margin, about 13 blocks at `caves.biomes.depthScale` 128), with m = `aquifer.abyssMargin` in C′ and E′ units. There a water status yields **barrier rock** where it would place water (y at or below the level) and air elsewhere, so water never touches abyss air from above or from the side.
- Task 6 measures m; Task 13 writes the defaults into the schema. m is set from the fields' measured gradient, so that the lateral margin is at least 6 blocks (the jitter's 2 plus 4) at the 95th percentile of the abyss's side positions: **measured 0.06** by the plan's Task 6 (the 95th percentile of the gradient 0.00956 per block, × 6 = 0.0573, rounded up to 0.005; Exit evidence, "The abyss margin (Task 6)"; the estimate was about 0.03); D31 records water voxels next to an abyss voxel holding air (`A5.abyssWaterFace`; lever: m up).
- Amends master :760: "Aquifers are disabled where …" → "aquifer water is disabled in the abyss and becomes barrier rock in a margin around it on every side (the depth margin 0.1 and a lateral margin); lava is not affected".
- Expected cost: rock plugs appear only where a flooded cell meets the margin (**estimate**: rare; D31 counts barrier voxels in the margin).

### 1.7 The order in T and sky-open water (D17, D19)

- After the cave-biome step (`terrainStage.ts:261`), the aquifer step replaces water v0 (`:263`): per non-solid voxel, untagged: water iff y ≤ ⌊level⌋ (the nearest-corner `surfaceWaterLevel`, `:148`); tagged: the aquifer (§1.2-§1.6) (D19). It writes the water scratch (`WATER_V`) the surface pass reads (`:266`) and a fluid type and level per voxel; barriers set `SOLID` and clear `TAG`; it tracks each position's highest fluid voxel (for aux A). `top` means the highest solid voxel (`TOP`, `:104`) everywhere in this spec; water v0's island base (`BASE`, `:107`) retires with it, and where a rule needs the ground under floating islands it says `groundTop` (the top of the highest solid run whose bottom lies below 181, SP3e's rule, `:160-171`).
- The surface pass then sees aquifer water (rule 4's branches, §5) and barrier rock (skinned as rock).
- **Seed sections**, after the springs step (D17), so springs are seeds too: a fluid voxel whose voxel below, or a horizontal neighbour inside the column, is passable with no fluid is an in-column seed. T writes no bit in the fluid byte (the proto carries no bit 6); it records in aux B a 24-bit mask, `seedSections`, of the sections holding at least one seed (4 bytes per column), and D rescans those sections (§4.2). This replaces master :764's flagging of voxels, so bit 6 in the final set marks only truncated fronts and contacts and `generatedAt` never reads a seed mark (§9.3). Seeds whose dry neighbour lies across a column border are D's boundary scan's (§4.2).
- The surface probe (`src/gen/surface/probe.ts:20`, `:29`, `:71`), `terrainSurfaceDebug` (`terrainStage.ts:330-341`) and the unit tests that pin v0 (`test/unit/terrainStage.test.ts:844-926`, `test/unit/surfaceProbe.test.ts:219-223`) follow; `test/unit/wetCover.test.ts` is re-written for D24.
- Aux A: `oceanFloorWG = top + 1`, unchanged; `worldSurfaceWG = max(top, the position's highest fluid voxel) + 1`, from the tracked maximum instead of the proof at `:284-289` (a tagged voxel above `top` can hold aquifer water, D19). Spring sources (§3) sit inside rock faces, at or below `top`.

### 1.8 The fast path and cost

Per T job, cell statuses are computed lazily and cached for the column's neighbourhood (3 × 35 × 3 cells: i cx − 1 … cx + 1, j −7 … 27, k cz − 1 … cz + 1, since j = ⌊(y + 1)/12⌋ runs −6 … 26 over y −63 … 319 and the candidates reach one row beyond; Spec correction 14), with each neighbourhood's maximum level; a voxel above it is air without a distance computation (master :762). The bulk is sky air above the levels and solid rock; the work is the tagged non-solid voxels below the levels (cave air: about 2.3k per column, the SP6b spec §1.5). **Estimate:** statuses 0.2-0.4 ms (Nf, Nspread per cell; Nlava per 64 × 40 × 64 block), voxels 0.3-0.6 ms (12 distances, a barrier noise where two statuses differ), the seed-section scan over every fluid voxel 0.05-0.1 ms (an ocean column holds up to about 25k water voxels; §1.7) and the springs with their border probes < 0.05 ms (§3.5), less v0's two 98,304-voxel loops: net +0.6-1.15 ms of `terrain.real` p50 (9.89 → about 10.5-11.0) and +1.6-2.7 ms p99 (16.92 → about 18.5-19.6, against 20: a margin of about 0.4 ms, which is why D31's "> 19 ms" trigger is checked first). The bench row `aquifer.column` (the step alone over the T candidates' columns) is gated at master's 1.0 / 2.5 ms (:1665). Levers in order (D31): the status cache per region instead of per job; skipping voxels whose 4 nearest cells are all empty and non-lava; the barrier noise only within the d2 − d1 band.

### 1.9 Parameters

A new `aquifer` group, Terrain scope (master §5.1 lists "aquifer", :1330), on the Parameters panel after Caves: `aquifer.enabled` (true); `surfaceBand` 12; `thresholds.{fullInland 0.8, fullOcean −0.3, partialInland 0.4, partialOcean −0.8}`; `lavaMaxLevel` −10; `lavaThreshold` 0.3; `lavaLevel` −50 (−54 until D31: Task 27n, the user's choice, rulings R23); `barrierBand` 30 (25 until D31: Task 27l's lever, rulings R21); `abyssMargin` 0.06 (§1.6, measured by the plan's Task 6; the estimate was 0.03); `noises.{floodedness, spread, lava, barrier}` (3D; λ and octaves from Minecraft's, **MC (memory)**: floodedness λ 128, spread λ 32, lava λ 2, barrier λ 8, one octave each; Task 6 measures m; Task 13 writes the defaults into the schema; tuned at D31). That is 15 leaves, each noise counting as one leaf as U2 counts `caves.noises.floor` (`test/unit/fixtures/liveness.ts:37-40`). Each leaf is live by U2 (§13.7), decided in the commit that adds it (Spec correction 5). `aquifer.enabled = false` gives dry cave air (sky-open water unchanged), a useful A/B in the panel.

## 2. Caves under water and the wet roof (D5, D24; Point 3)

- `wetMaskOf` (`src/gen/column/columnPoint.ts:62`) reads the lake level instead of `surfaceWaterLevel`: 1 inside a lake mask, 0 elsewhere. The wet roof's caps (g_w, S_w) and the carvers' σ_w then close caves within 20 blocks of lake beds only; under the sea and rivers, caves are as open as anywhere (SP6a's families and carvers, unchanged in shape). Master :691's paragraph becomes "the lake roof", :742-743's "carvers shrink to nothing under water" becomes "under lakes".
- `caves.limits.waterDepth` (16, `src/core/params/schema.ts:302`) stays: the lake roof's depth (O17's "decides `caves.limits.waterDepth`").
- Under the sea, cells near the surface take 63 (§1.2) and deep cells flood readily (§1.3), so caves under the sea are flooded; under rivers, near-surface cells take 63 and deeper ones follow the inland thresholds, so a river's cave flooding stops a cell or two down, behind a barrier or a settle spill.
- The SP6a wet-roof unit tests and the ~20 files that pin `wetMask` or `waterDepth` (recon-gen §4) keep passing except where they assert sea or river closure; those assertions move to lakes. `wetCover` (`test/unit/wetCover.test.ts:22`, `:92`) becomes "every lake position has wet′ = 1".
- **Under Point 3's alternative:** the wet terms leave the three built-in density trees (a density-tree map in `MIGRATIONS[4]` with a `gv9-density-trees.json` fixture), σ_w leaves the carvers, `caves.limits.waterDepth` is deleted (an edited tree's `ref` to it is rewritten to its stored value as a `const`), and the lake guard of master :631 is gone.

## 3. Springs (D1-D4, D21)

### 3.1 The common rule

A spring is a source voxel placed in a solid voxel of the proto: the voxel becomes AIR plus `WATER_SOURCE` or `LAVA_SOURCE` (master :334). Per chunk (cx, cz) and kind k, `Xoshiro128(hash2(deriveSeed(seed, 'springs.' + k), cx, cz))` draws `springs.k.tries` tries in a fixed order, each (x, z) uniform in the chunk, a direction d of the four horizontal ones and u ∈ [0, 1); the try picks a height y from its kind's range (below) and is accepted when:
- the spring voxel is solid with a state in the kind's valid set (water: every `CARVABLE` terrain state except sand, red_sand and gravel; lava: deepslate and the five abyss rocks), not a spring already, and not tagged;
- the voxels above and below it and the two lateral and one back neighbours are solid (`terrainSolidAt`);
- the outlet (the neighbour in d) is non-solid with no fluid, and the `minFall` voxels below the outlet are non-solid with no fluid.
Reads inside the column use T's arrays as they stand before any spring is written; reads outside it use the point functions (`terrainSolidAt`, `fluidAt`, `caveTagAt`, `caveBiomeAt`), equal to bulk (DT2) and blind to springs too, so the decision is identical from any column and no border ring is excluded. Only tries near a border need probes (about 1 in 8, ≤ `minFall` + 4 probes each). Accepted springs are written after the surface pass (D17), so the voxels around a spring keep the skin of a solid run. Because no try sees another spring, two springs can end up side by side (one in the other's lateral or back face), which makes each two-faced; this is accepted as rare (**estimate**: under 1 per 10 km²) and counted (`A5.adjacentSprings`, recorded), rather than having every border try replay the neighbour's tries.

### 3.2 Water springs in mountain faces (D1, D2)

Range: yLo = ⌈`offset` at the outlet⌉ + `springs.water.minFall` (7: 8 until Task 28a, §11's first lever against truncated streams), the outlet's 2D surface estimate read by `readFieldHalo` (the SP6b spec §1.2; exact for positions within 4 blocks of the column); yHi = `top` − 2 (with `islands.enabled`, `groundTop` − 2, §1.7, so island faces are left to §3.3); y = yLo + ⌊u·(yHi − yLo + 1)⌋, rejected if yHi < yLo. No y floor (the plan's Task 19, a Spec correction: the `springs.water.minY` (64) first written here is redundant, since a land outlet's `offset` is at least about 63 and so yLo ≥ about 71; it could bind only on a try beside the sea or a river with a dry 8-block fall, which U2's mountain witness and 11,000 targeted columns never find, so the leaf could not be live by U2; dropping it changes no generated block; a floor can come back with a live witness if D31's levers bring springs down to the sea's band). With the outlet above its column's surface estimate by ≥ `minFall` (7; 8 until Task 28a), the fall is open to the sky in practice; A5 measures the fall on the settled world and counts a waterfall as **visible** when the outlet has no solid voxel above it up to y 319 and its straight fall is ≥ 8 (§13.1; so a fall of exactly `minFall` 7 is accepted but not counted visible). A try the rule accepts is still rejected when its fall is predicted to end truncated (§11's last lever, Task 28d, taken by Task 31r, rulings R25): `predictTruncated` runs the settle's kernel (§4.1, `settleWindow`, the same rules and caps as D's settle and the global reference, so water enters abyss air as rulings R23 has it) over the point probes within 17 blocks of the would-be source, seeded with that source alone, and rejects the try when a voxel of its lone front carries bit 6 (the reading `A5.springsTruncated` counts); the probes are blind to springs, so the decision is the same from any column (D21). Default `springs.water.tries` 18 (3, the **estimate** for Point 2's 50 per km², until Task 31r; the plan's Spec correction 6: the 6 first written here was sized for Point 2 (b) before the user's answer; with the rejection above, 18 = round(3 × 50 / 8.5) by 27b's rule, the user's choice, rulings R25; §3.5; the readings in Exit evidence, Task 31r).

### 3.3 Island-edge springs (D3)

With `islands.enabled`: the range is an island run of the column (a solid run whose bottom ≥ 181, SP3e's runs): the run is the k-th island run where k = ⌊u·runs⌋, and y uniform in [bottom + 1, top − 1] of that run from a second draw; outlet and fall as §3.1 with a least fall of 8, the constant `ISLAND_SPRING_MIN_FALL` (`src/core/constants.ts`). The fall below an island's edge reaches the ground or the sea (often 100-200 blocks; falling is unlimited, master :924). Default `springs.island.tries` 4 (**estimate**). No `springs.island.minFall` leaf (the plan's Task 20, a Spec correction: the leaf first written here only gates the fall, which has no role in the island range, and island-edge falls run 50-135 blocks, so U2's steps 7 and 9 changed no column in 12,000 floating_islands island columns and the leaf could not be live by U2; the constant 8 changes no generated block).

### 3.4 Lava springs in the abyss (D4)

Range: yLo = −55 + `springs.lava.minFall` (4), yHi = min(`top` − 2, ⌊Y0(x, z) − `caves.biomes.depthScale`·`caves.biomes.abyss.depthMin`⌋) (the abyss's top at that position, the picker's own inputs); the outlet must be tagged cave air whose nibble is the abyss (3) with no fluid. The fall then spreads about 3 blocks where it lands (§4.1). Water from above may reach the same floor (a water spring's stream or a lake's spill falling into abyss air, rulings R23): where it meets this lava, §4.1's merge decides (lava wins, the contact flagged for SP7b). Default `springs.lava.tries` 16 (**estimate**; abyss air is a small share of each column's height, so many tries miss).

### 3.5 Parameters and cost

A new `springs` group, Terrain scope: `springs.water.{tries 18, minFall 7}` (minFall 8 → 7 by Task 28a, §11's first lever, `A5.springsTruncated` 0.6 → 0.5 on the presets group; tries 3, the starting **estimate** for Point 2's 50 per km², half the 6 first sized for 100, until Task 31r; Task 27l's step to 5 was measured and not taken, rulings R22: 30.3 per km² at 3 on §13.1's steep-land sample; 3 → 18 with Task 28d's rejection of a predicted truncation, §3.2, by Task 31r, the user's choice, rulings R25; no `minY`, §3.2's Spec correction), `springs.island.{tries 4}` (no `minFall`: the constant `ISLAND_SPRING_MIN_FALL` 8, §3.3's Spec correction), `springs.lava.{tries 16, minFall 4}`: 5 leaves. Terrain scope means an edit re-runs T (the decorate-scope Apply path stays SP8b's, master :1973). Cost **estimate**: 26 tries per chunk, a few array reads each and about 3 tries per chunk with probes: < 0.05 ms per column (at the first defaults; since Task 31r, 38 tries per chunk, and each try the common rule accepts runs the prediction's settle over its probes; measured by `terrain.real`, Exit evidence, Task 31r).

## 4. The gen-time settle (D22, D23; master §3.14)

### 4.1 Rules (`fluidRules.ts`)

Fluid states use the frozen byte (`src/world/blocks/fluid.ts:1-27`): level 0 source … 7, bit 3 falling. For a fluid f (water: levels 0 … 7, +1 per block, search 4; lava: levels 0, 2, 4, 6, +2 per block, so 3 blocks of reach, search 2, Minecraft's overworld lava, **MC (memory)**; the byte stores the level as Minecraft writes it, so both fluids share §6.1's heights):
- **Two fixed points.** Water and lava settle independently: each fluid's settle treats every voxel holding the other type's proto fluid as `block`. The two results are then merged per voxel: a voxel reached by both takes lava with `unsettled` = 1 (a contact, which SP7b's reactions resolve), and every other voxel keeps its own type's result. The merge reads one voxel of each result, so it is local and order-free. (Neither type reads the other's settled result, which would make the first front processed win a contested voxel: the fixed point would not be unique, and two receivers could resolve one contact differently.)
- **Falling:** a fluid voxel whose voxel below is passable (non-solid or `hold`/`displace`) makes it falling f (falling bit, level 0), unless the proto holds a source of f there; falls are unlimited.
- **Spreading:** a source spreads sideways always; a flowing or falling voxel spreads sideways only when its voxel below is not passable or holds f **in the proto** (a stream reaching a lake or the sea spreads over it, as in Minecraft; a stream reaching water the settle itself placed falls into it, which makes that voxel falling, as Minecraft's falling water does). The spread goes to the open sides at minimal distance to the nearest **drop** (a searched voxel whose voxel below is open for f, that is passable and not holding the other type's proto fluid, f's own proto fluid there included) within the search reach, by a breadth-first search over passable voxels that hold no proto source of f: a source of f is neither a first side nor walked through (flowing and falling proto bytes are), as in Minecraft's slope search, **MC (memory)**; with no drop in reach, to every open side (f's own sources among them, which changes nothing). So a lake's or the sea's shore source searches only its dry side, and spreads down a bank or coast step within reach instead of standing as a wall (Spec correction, rulings R21: Task 25 found that a search walking through the fluid's own sources finds them, over water, as drops at distance 1). A voxel reached sideways from level k carries k + the fluid's step (a landed fall spreads as a source would), up to level 7 (water) or 6 (lava).
- **New sources:** a passable voxel with ≥ 2 horizontal water sources **of the proto** beside it, over a solid voxel or a proto water source, becomes a water source (water only). Minecraft's rule reads any source; the settle makes no source except by this rule, so reading the proto only drops the cascade of rule-made sources across a flat floor, which is what would make the reach unbounded.
- **Order:** a voxel's settled value is the least any feeder gives it, in the order source < falling < flowing 1 < … < 7 < dry. Every condition that reads fluid reads the proto, and the passability and drop tests read blocks, so each rule can only lower a value when its feeders' values fall: the rules are monotone, and the settled world is their least fixed point from the proto, unique whatever the processing order.
- **Distance:** every fluid voxel carries a horizontal distance per value: 0 for a proto source's own value, and for each value k the least, over the derivations that give the voxel k, of the feeder's distance at the value it spreads + 1 for a sideways step (+0 for a fall). A derivation of k is kept only when its distance is below the voxel's distance at every value ≤ k (a lower value no farther from its source already does everything it would), so the per-value distances only fall and the rules stay monotone. The voxel's value is its least (Order) and its distance is that value's, so its level and its distance come from one derivation, and the cap reads the distance of the level that spreads (a voxel fed at level 2 from 9 blocks along a corridor, with a fall on the way, and at level 3 from 3 blocks across has level 2 and distance 9, and its level-3 derivation still spreads level 4 from distance 3; without a fall a water level is at most its distance, since both start at 0 and rise by 1 per sideways step: the plan's Spec correction 13). Taking the distance independently of the level would let a level travel along one feeder's path while the cap counts along another's, past the radius below. A source made by the new-source rule takes the least of its feeders' distances + 1, so the count never restarts. A voxel at the cap, **12** (water) or **14** (lava), does not spread sideways; it keeps `unsettled` = 1 when its spread rule names a passable target (a test on blocks only, so it reads 1 block; the target may already hold fluid, in which case the flag is a harmless wake-up for SP7b; master :931).
- **Radius:** every read that decides a voxel v lies on or beside a feeding path from a proto source to v: the passability and proto fluid of the path's voxels and of the voxels below them, the nearest-drop search up to 4 (2) blocks beyond each path voxel (its blocks, and the proto fluid at its searched voxels and below them: the skip of f's own sources, rulings R21, reads the same voxels the other type's `block` test already reads, so it adds no read beyond this radius), and the new-source rule's proto reads 1 block beyond. The distance of the voxel's value grows by one per sideways step along the path that derived it, so each path voxel lies within the cap of v horizontally, and every read lies within cap + search = 12 + 4 = 16 (water) or 14 + 2 = 16 (lava) blocks of v (the new-source rule's 1 block and the `unsettled` test's 1 block are within the search's reach). K's 3 × 3 extends 16 blocks beyond K on every side, so every derivation of a voxel of K reads only the window, and the windowed result on K equals a global settle. This is the proof; §13.4's `DT1.settleReference` measures it.
- **The abyss is no exception:** a water front enters a voxel whose cave nibble is the abyss (3) as any other, under the rules above (the fall, the spread, the caps, the nearest-drop search, which searches abyss air too; the other type's proto lava stays `block` for water, and the merge decides the contacts). The aquifer still writes no water there (§1.6), so the abyss's water is only what the settle brings (`A5.waterIntoAbyss`, §13.1). (Spec correction, rulings R23: the rule "a water front never enters a voxel whose cave nibble is the abyss; that front ends there without `unsettled`" is withdrawn, the user's choice after Task 27m's shots of streams cut at the abyss's edge; no rule reads the nibble now, so the radius below is unchanged.)
The same functions are SP7b's sim's single-step rules (its tick applies them to a scheduled voxel); SP7a unit-tests the converged shapes (F1's diamond of radius 7, falls, infinite sources, the cap).

### 4.2 Where it runs: D's step 2 (D23)

D exists as SP4a's skeleton (`src/gen/pipeline/decorateStage.ts:30-34`, `:108-145`), runs once K's 3 × 3 is at Proto (master :1002) and iterates the steps placing nothing. SP7a adds, in master :889-895's order: step 1 places nothing new (springs are already proto sources, D21; SP8b's lava lakes will add theirs here); **step 2, settle**; steps 3-4 nothing; step 5 the diff overlay, now rebuilt from the settled output; step 6 the final heightmaps from final blocks and fluid (`finalHeightmaps.ts:1-11`, so WORLD_SURFACE and MOTION_BLOCKING count the waterfalls). The minimal machinery:
- **Seeds:** the in-column seeds of the 9 columns, found by rescanning the sections each column's aux B `seedSections` names (§1.7), plus the cross-column seeds, found by scanning the 12 boundaries between the 3 × 3's columns (a fluid voxel beside a passable fluid-free voxel across the boundary), skipping uniform section pairs (an all-water or all-air section pair has no seed). Every proto fluid voxel is a source (distance 0), and a front travels at most the cap, so seeds farther than 12 (water) or 14 (lava) horizontally from K are skipped.
- **Reads** through `NeighborhoodReader.proto(dx, dz)`'s section views (`src/world/store/api.ts:207-211`); state held in arrays sized by the visits (no full-height region copy, recon-gen §7's 48² × 384).
- **Writes:** K's sections whose fluid changed, copy-on-write (`replaceFluid`, keeping blocks and the cave entry); bit 6 in the output marks only truncated fronts and contacts (§4.1). The proto carries no bit 6 (§1.7), so a section the settle leaves unchanged shares the proto's fluid slot (`shareFinal`, `decorateStage.ts:126-128`) without carrying any seed mark into the final set.
- **The generated-fluid layer:** the same changed sections' settled fluid, kept as the column's generated value (§9.3).
- **Budget:** visits per job recorded on the D bench row (`decorate.column`, the settle and the skeleton), with master's 20k visits and D ≤ 3 / 8 ms as targets (:933, :1668); a miss goes to D31's levers (barriers wider, `aquifer.barrierBand` 25 → 30; fewer border contacts), never to a semantic budget (a visit cap would make the result depend on the window). Each seed is settled by every receiver it can reach (≤ 4 columns at cap 12 for most seeds); recorded.
- `stop()` is polled between fronts as between steps (the skeleton's 91 polls plus one per 1,024 visits).

### 4.3 Consistency

Master §3.16's settle row (:958) is restated: "3 × 3 proto (with springs) | 3 × 3 proto | own final + the generated-fluid layer | every fluid-deciding read from the proto; cap + search ≤ 16 blocks (12 + 4 for water, 14 + 2 for lava)". §4.1's radius argument proves it; DT1's `settleReference` and `settleIsolated` (§13.4) measure it; `DT1.light` already hashes final fluid over threads and orders (`test/metrics/light.metric.ts:61`, `:146-169`).

### 4.4 Truncation and SP7b

A truncated front keeps `unsettled`; nothing reads the bit in SP7a (mesher, light and walk ignore it, recon-render §14). SP7b's sim never ticks it until an edit's update touches it (master :931, master D18). A2 bounds the share (§13.1). If A2 cannot be met with the levers, master's fallback (policy B, natural ticks with a lineage bit, :1760) needs the sim, so it goes to the user with the measurements and SP7b (O80).

## 5. Rule 4's under-water branches (D12, D25)

- In `root.rules[2]` (rule 4; the builder's comment naming it is `src/core/params/defaultTrees.ts:380-384`) of both built-in trees, two leaves before the lush floor branch, written as nested `condition`s (the surface DSL has no `and`, `src/gen/surface/rules.ts:85`): `condition(caveBiome{floor, [lush]}, condition(not(water{offset 0, runTop true}), condition(stoneDepth{side floor, offset 1, addSurfaceDepth false}, block clay)))` (two deep) and `condition(caveBiome{floor, [plain, dripstone]}, condition(not(water{offset 0, runTop true}), condition(stoneDepth{side floor, offset 0, addSurfaceDepth false}, block gravel)))` (one deep) (the `water` condition holds when no water is directly above the run's top, `src/gen/surface/conditions.ts:47-49`; `runTop` true reads the run's top). The abyss has no water (D4), so no branch for it.
- The scan's `waterAbove`/`waterTop` come from the water scratch, now the aquifer's (§1.7; `src/gen/surface/scan.ts:111-114`, `:237-264`).
- The fast path's water-gated class (D25), fuzzed against full evaluation over trees with `water` gates and random aquifer columns (`src/gen/surface/compile.ts:12-26`'s fuzz). `RULES_COLUMN_COST_MAX` unchanged.
- Settle's water (D) is invisible to the rules (they run in T): floors under a spring's spill keep their dry skin. Stated, not fixed (a D-side re-skin is SP8b's kind of work).

## 6. The flowing mesher (D9, D26, D27)

### 6.1 Heights

A fluid voxel's height h (one function in `src/world/blocks/fluidRules.ts`, shared by the mesher, the frame step's eye test, D29, and the walk, §9.2): 1 if the same fluid is directly above or it is falling; else (8 − level)/9 (a source 8/9; lava's levels 0, 2, 4, 6 give 8/9, 6/9, 4/9, 2/9). A top corner's height (**MC (memory)**, Minecraft's average): over the up to 4 voxels around the corner, if any holds the same fluid with the same fluid above, 1; else the weighted mean of their heights, a voxel with h ≥ 0.8 weighing 10 and any other fluid voxel of that type weighing 1, a passable fluid-free voxel counting height 0 with weight 1, a solid voxel not counted. `cornerDrop` = round((1 − h)·9), clamped to 0 … 8 (the unit stays ninths, master :1069; a source surface stays drop 1). Each corner's value is a function of its 4 voxels only, so neighbouring quads share their corners exactly (no cracks).

### 6.2 Faces

- **Top:** drawn when the same fluid is not above. Four corner drops; flat quads (four equal drops) merge greedily as today; sloped quads are one per voxel. The split diagonal is the one joining the two corners with the lower summed height (a fixed rule from the corner values, so neighbours agree).
- **Sides against non-fluid, non-occluding neighbours:** from the voxel's bottom to its two top corners' heights, as today (`src/mesh/fluid.ts:139-156`).
- **Sides between two voxels of the same fluid** (new; amends master :1060's "only against non-fluid neighbours", and `:146`'s `nt === ft` skip): when the neighbour's top corners on the shared edge are lower than this voxel's, the strip between them: bottom vertices at the neighbour's corner drops, top vertices at this voxel's, both encoded as `cornerDrop` from the voxel's top (positions at y + 1). R1's brute force (`test/harness/refs.ts:125-126`, `:155`) adds the same rule.
- **Falling:** full height; sides full.
- **Water beside lava** (new): today water skips its side faces against lava because "lava's opaque full-height face covers every plane the two share" (`src/mesh/fluid.ts:7-8`, `:154-156`). With lava sources at 8/9 and flowing lava down to 2/9 (D9), lava's side reaches only its corner heights, which would leave a see-through band. So water draws a side strip against lava from lava's corner heights on the shared edge up to its own, where its own are higher (the same form as the strips between one fluid); lava draws its sides against water from its bottom to its corner heights, as against any non-lava neighbour. Water's up face under lava stays as today. R1's brute force (`test/harness/refs.ts:155`, which already counts water faces against lava as visible) adds the strip rule in lockstep.
- **Depth:** `fluidDepths` (`:196-260`) unchanged (the vertical run, clamped to 15); a falling column joins the pool below as today.

### 6.3 Lava (D26)

Lava stays in pass 0 with the solid word (`src/mesh/greedy.ts:69`); its four corners' drops go in the `flags` nibble (bits 26-29, unused by any shader, `src/mesh/packing.ts:6-8`) when `layer` is `LAVA_LAYER`; the terrain shader subtracts `flags · 8/9` eighths for that layer as the water shader does (`src/render/materials/glsl/water.glsl.ts:26-27`). Lava's emissive look stays block light 15; SP8a's emissive flag for lava is implied by its layer. Side strips and the split rule as water. Lava sources at 8/9 (D9).

### 6.4 Light and AO

Smooth light per vertex, AO off, as today (`src/mesh/fluid.ts:72-73`, `:163`).

### 6.5 R5 (D27)

- **Definition:** water quads on top faces of surface voxels (no same fluid above) over the distinct surface top faces, Medium meshing, on named water regions: fast a 4 × 4-chunk coast window (fast's regions hold no water today, `test/metrics/.out/R5.json` 0 / 0), quick and full the current sites plus a coast and a lake window (§13.3); sloped quads are in the gated ratio and also recorded apart (`R5.slopedShare`).
- **The measuring task first:** today's 0.2236 (a local, gitignored file; see the header's note on figures) re-read under the definition and split by face (top, side, down) and by key (`depth`, `cornerDrop`, light).
- **Levers in order** (the definition above is D27's from the start, not a lever; it removes sides and down faces from the reading): (1) `depth` quantised to 4 bands (0-1, 2-3, 4-7, 8-15) in the top face's merge key (`src/mesh/fluid.ts`, `TOP_DEPTH`), the top face's `depth` field written as the band's rounded middle (1, 3, 6, 12), so the Fast colour takes it (a visible colour step at band edges, judged in the shots); side and down faces keep the exact depth; (2) dropped (Spec correction 16, Task 34): uniform-light tops with equal light already merge under today's key B; merging unequal lights would mis-light the quad (`emit` takes every vertex's light from the merged key). Gated ≤ 0.1 from the commit that reaches it (`activeFrom` 'SP7a'). A miss after Lever 1 goes to the user with the measurements; a further lever must name a key change that merges faces whose key-B words differ, with its visible cost judged in a shot.

### 6.6 The Firefox look

RegionMesh sorts translucent arenas by region only (`src/render/regionMesh.ts:733-760`) and water writes depth, so a waterfall in front of a pool in the same region can hide the pool behind it in Firefox. SP7a records it in the Firefox run (a waterfall review link) and hands a per-section sort to SP11 if the user finds it visible (D37). Chrome sorts sections (`src/render/regionBatch.ts:355-357`, `:535-539`).

## 7. Water tiers (D10, D28; master §4.10)

- **Fast:** as today (alpha 0.72, shallow-to-deep by `depth`, `src/render/materials/glsl/water.glsl.ts:46-48`).
- **Fancy** adds: Schlick Fresnel with F0 0.02 toward `skyColor(reflect(view, n))` (`src/render/materials/glsl/common.glsl.ts:48`, shared with the sky); two octaves of scrolling procedural ripple normals (value-noise gradients at λ 4 and 1.7 blocks, scrolling at 0.4 and 0.9 blocks/s in two directions; no texture); on sloped surfaces the normal from `dFdx`/`dFdy` of the world position, ripples added on it; alpha 0.72 → 0.6 + 0.3·Fresnel. Vertex waves: SP11. Animated texture: SP8a/SP11.
- **Uniforms:** `waterTier` (0 Fast, 1 Fancy), `timeSeconds` (a new frame uniform, wrapped every 3,600 s to keep precision; `FrameState.t` is the time of day, `src/render/frameState.ts:8-9`).
- **Settings:** `WorldSettings.water: 'fast' | 'fancy'` (`src/ui/settings.ts:9-28`, normalised; default 'fancy', Medium's per master :1261), a "Water" select row in Settings › Graphics, live; `GraphicsPreset.water` widens to `'fast' | 'fancy'` (`src/core/params/graphicsPresets.ts:15-16`) and Medium's value becomes `'fancy'` (`:30`).
- **Cost:** master's "water (Fancy) ≤ 1.2 ms" at Medium (:1706), measured by the bench's water segment (D36: frame GPU time at a coast with Fancy minus Fast, p50).

## 8. Underwater and lava fog (D11, D29; master :1113)

- The frame step sets `eyeFluid` from the eye voxel's fluid byte and §6.1's surface height; the teleport resets it (`src/ui/world/teleport.ts:162`).
- `FOG_GLSL` (`src/render/materials/glsl/common.glsl.ts:66-77`) branches on an `eyeFluid` uniform: in water the fog factor is f = 1 − 2^(−8·(d/L)²) with L = 56 blocks (less than 1/256 of the colour left at 56 blocks), its colour the Fast deep colour × `lightmapAt(0, eyeSky)`; in lava the same form with L = 3 blocks and the colour [255, 90, 20] × 0.6. The tint: the fog colour mixed at 0.25 into every fragment while the eye is in a fluid.
- The sky dome's fragment (`src/render/materials/glsl/sky.glsl.ts`) applies the underwater fog at d = fogEnd, so the sky seen through the surface is tinted.
- The X-ray material has no fog (`src/render/materials/uniforms.ts:124-141`) and the X-ray view hides terrain and water, so nothing changes with the X-ray on.
- Night vision keeps lifting the lightmap; the fog colour follows the lightmap.
- Zero allocation per frame (the frame step's pattern, `test/unit/zeroAlloc.test.ts`).

## 9. Light, physics and editing

### 9.1 Light (D14)

Lava emits 15 at any level (`src/world/blocks/light.ts:37-46`); generated lava opens L's block-light scan by itself (`anyLava`, `src/light/initial.ts:161-177`, `:242`), so `REC_DIFF_FLAG` needs no change (O37: verified). With aquifer and abyss lava, many deep regions run the block BFS: `light.column` gains a lava twin, `light.column.lava`, on a region holding generated lava (§13.5). L1 (`test/thresholds.ts`) re-asserted with lava emitters; L2's `abyssLit` (recorded) turns positive by design. Water's opacity 1 per voxel stays: a flooded cave out of the sky's reach is dark unless lava is near.

### 9.2 Walking (D15)

The body is in a fluid when, for an overlapped voxel holding it, (y + that voxel's surface height) − feetY > `FLUID_WADE_DEPTH` (0.25 block, `src/core/constants.ts`; §6.1's `surfaceHeight`, in `src/world/blocks/fluidRules.ts`: the player layer may import `world/` but not `gen/`, `test/arch/rules/imports.ts:30`). "The body's lowest point below the surface height" alone would not do (Spec correction 15): a body walking on the ground has its feet at the film voxel's y + 0, below any surface height above 0, so a level-7 film (h 1/9) would still swim. With the wade depth a body on the ground walks through water levels 6-7 and lava level 6 (h ≤ 2/9) and swims from water level 5 (h 3/9) up (a falling voxel, or one with the same fluid above, has h 1); swimming, drag and lava damage as SP5a's otherwise (`src/player/walk.ts:192-266`). The current's push is SP7b's (it needs flow directions the sim maintains). Physics unit tests (master :1483) re-run with thin films and falls.

### 9.3 Edits and the generated value (D23)

`generatedAt` (`src/sim/edits.ts:315`, `:337-343`, `:696`, `:708`, `:819`) reads blocks from the proto and fluid from the generated-fluid layer where it holds the section, else the proto; D's overlay rebuild (`decorateStage.ts:59-82`, `:126-137`) starts from the same. So an edit that restores a voxel to its settled value removes its diff entry, and a reload reproduces the runtime world (E3, E5 re-run). Saved diffs are absolute (`src/sim/diffStore.ts:1-6`): an SP6b world's dry-air edit inside a cave that floods in SP7a stays a dry pocket with still water around it (no sim until SP7b), stated in §15.4. A paste of a generated waterfall pastes frozen flowing water (SP5c D9; SP7b schedules its ticks).

## 10. The X-ray, the map, the slice and the probe (D13)

### 10.1 The X-ray

Flooded caves are already hollow (tagged, `air` plus fluid, recon-render §11). The `xray` word gains `fluid` in bits 11-12 (0 dry, 1 water, 2 lava; bits 11-31 are spare, `src/mesh/packing.ts:11-13`): the fluid of the hollow voxel each wall face bounds; the merge key adds it. The X-ray material mixes the kind or biome colour toward water blue or lava orange by 0.5 (a uniform). The legend gains "Flooded" and "Lava". R1's `xray` brute force (`bruteXrayFaces`, `test/harness/refs.ts:190`) adds the fluid; a new part `R1.xrayFluid` (faces whose fluid bits ≠ their hollow voxel's fluid type) is 0 by construction.

### 10.2 The slice and the map

- The `caves` field (`src/gen/inspect/fieldSlice.ts:496-540`) evaluates `aquifer.fluidAt` at its carved samples only (tag ≠ 0; about a tenth of the samples) and draws water and lava; its values gain, per sample, the fluid type (`fluid`: 0 dry, 1 water, 2 lava) and the cell its rule reads (`aquifer`: `kind << 24 | status`, the packed status and `cellKindOf`'s kind from `fluidAt`'s `statusOut`, rulings P39; `AQUIFER_EMPTY` off the carved samples), in place of `fluid: null` (Spec correction 9); `slice.caves128` (280.3 ms against 300, recon-render §11) gains about 2-5 ms (**estimate**; gated at 300). T gives tagged voxels the aquifer's fluid too (D19), so the field matches T's proto fluid at its samples; springs and settle's water are not shown (stated in the legend's help).
- The `caves` field's hover (the slice's own codes, `src/ui/inspector/sliceModel.ts:553`, not `fieldProbe`, which serves `density`, `node` and `rule` only: Spec correction 9) adds to a carved sample's kind its fluid and its cell's level and kind, read from the values above: "water (aquifer level 63, near-surface)", "water (aquifer level 63, flooded)", "water (aquifer level −23, partial)", "lava (aquifer level −23, lava)" or "dry (aquifer empty)" (a partial cell at level 63 reads "partial"; the kind tells it from a flooded one): a part of master §5.5's probe "aquifer cell / level / status" (:1378); the crosshair probe and the "aquifer level and status" field go to SP10.
- The `blocks` field and the Voxels mode read T's proto with fluid (`fieldSlice.ts:544-560`; `src/workers/sliceJob.ts:149-207`), so they show aquifer water and lava now (settle's water is D's and not shown, stated).
- `isWater` (`src/ui/inspector/sliceModel.ts:457`) becomes `fluidType === FLUID_WATER`; the legend lists lava (`:489-499`).
- The map: its cave slice square inherits the field; its water layers already read `surfaceWaterLevel` (`src/gen/map/layers.ts:56`, `:70`). An aquifer map layer is SP10's (master :2000).

## 11. Failure modes and review focus

- **Waterfalls everywhere or nowhere:** `A5.waterfalls` within Point 2's bounds; D31's screenshots; the lever `springs.water.tries`.
- **A grid of springs, or none on chunk borders:** probes at borders (D21); `A5.borderRatio` 0.8-1.25; `A5.clarkEvans` recorded.
- **A stream that stops in mid-slope** (a truncated front, the anti-goal "cortes abruptos"): `A5.springsTruncated` (its own gate, since A2's share is diluted by the oceans' volume) and A2's unsettled share ≤ 0.1 %; levers: `springs.water.minFall`, then yLo measured from the ground below the outlet, then barriers, then rejecting at placement a try whose fall is predicted to end with a truncated front (a probe of the landing within the cap); the shots. Each lever is applied while it still lowers the reading (the user's addition, §13.1).
- **A seam where a stream crosses a chunk border:** every fluid-deciding read from the proto and cap + search ≤ 16 (D22, §4.1's radius); `DT1.settleReference` 0 on windows with a water/lava contact and a stream over a pool; `DT1.settleIsolated` 0.
- **Water standing as a wall** (a truncated front; no abyss stop since rulings R23): `C7.waterWall` after settle with its new population (§13.2), recorded, not gated, in SP7a (the user, rulings R24 item 1: SP7b owns its final definition and bound; the user reviews the fronts in-world at Task 47, from §13.9's `wallFrontN` links); v0's walls and aquifer borders become flows; `terrainScatter`'s `waterWallShare` recorded (`test/harness/terrainScatter.ts:308-310`).
- **Dry caves flooded** (barriers too weak): A1 ≥ 40 %; lever: `aquifer.barrierBand`, then the inland thresholds.
- **Water generated in the abyss:** `A5.abyssWater` 0 (the aquifer's, by §1.6); the water the settle brings into it (rulings R23) recorded as `A5.waterIntoAbyss`.
- **No lava, or lava everywhere:** A3's parts; levers `aquifer.lavaThreshold`, `lavaMaxLevel`; the abyss's `A5.lavaFalls`.
- **Bedrock exposed:** `C7.bedrockExposed` 0 re-checked on every group (no floor change, §1.4).
- **B3 losing cave air to water:** B3 counts dry cave air (master :1516); `B3.abyssMin` (0.0320 on full, the SP6b spec's Threshold log) is reduced by lava pools and, a little, by the abyss air the settle's water may take (no aquifer water in the abyss, but a stream may fall into it; Spec correction, rulings R23): re-measured at D31; lever: `aquifer.lavaThreshold` 0.3 → 0.4, re-checking A3.
- **T over budget:** `aquifer.column` and the T gates; §1.8's levers.
- **D slow or visit-heavy:** `decorate.column` recorded; §4.2's levers.
- **Cracks or holes in flowing water:** corner values per corner (§6.1); R1 0 with side strips; the review shot of a spill.
- **R5 above 0.1:** §6.5's levers; a miss goes to the user.
- **Fancy over 1.2 ms:** the water segment; levers: one ripple octave, then Fancy off below a distance (a uniform).
- **Fog on the wrong side of the surface:** D29's height test; a unit test at y + 0.85 / 0.95 in a source.
- **Edits reverting after reload in settled water:** D23; E3 and E5 with an edit in a spill.
- **The probe disagreeing with T** (barriers): `DT2.probeBulk` with `terrainSolidAt`; `DT2.aquiferProbe` 0.
- **Saved worlds without the new branches:** D34's migration tests.
- **CI past 6 min:** §14's levers in order.

## 12. What is kept

SP6b's cave biomes, rock floor, X-ray modes, culling and UI; SP6a's cave shapes (the wet roof under lakes, D24); the column stage and every `surfaceWaterLevel` (`sp2a.*`, `sp3e.column.point.*` unchanged); rules 1-3 and rule 4's dry branches; the fluid byte, the vertex words' layouts (the fluid word unchanged; the solid word's `flags` read only for lava; the X-ray word's spare bits used); buckets placing still sources; SP5c's editing, clipboard and history; SP5b's persistence (`wi10` unchanged; diffs already carry the fluid byte and the tick trailer stays empty); every existing key, map layer and hash.

## 13. Metrics and tests

### 13.1 A1-A5 (D30)

Over the caves passes' 32 × 32-chunk regions (`test/metrics/caves.metric.ts`; six default regions, three preset regions, `cave_heavy`'s two and the biome region), each read after the global settle reference over the region's T dump (§13.4; parts on its interior, 2 chunks in from the edge). Each part is written to `.out` in its commit and gated at the tuning task (Task "tuning", §13.8) at its bound, `activeFrom` 'SP7a', unless it holds by construction (gated from its commit).
- **A1** `dryShare` (master :1528): among tagged non-solid voxels below y 63 not connected (3D, `components3d`, `test/harness/flood.ts:98`, through tagged non-solid voxels and fluid voxels only, all at y ≤ 62: sky air above land never links two caves) to sky-open water at positions whose `surfaceWaterLevel` is 63 (sea or river), so a cave with a sky entrance on land counts unless it reaches the sea or a river below the water line (master :1528's meaning), the share with no fluid; ≥ 40 %, per group, the worst; recorded per region and on T alone (before settle).
- **A2** `unsupported` and `unsettled` (:1529): fluid voxels the rules of §4.1 would change in one application (a level a feeder would lower, a missing fall, a spread into a passable voxel), counting unsettled ones, over all fluid voxels / the unsettled share; ≤ 0.1 % / ≤ 0.1 %, per group (the denominator is every fluid voxel, as master :1529 writes it; the guard on the springs themselves is `A5.springsTruncated`, which A2's pooled share cannot be: the controller's call on the builder check's question 4); recorded by origin (sky-open, aquifer, spring) and per km², with water/lava contacts (§4.1's merge) counted apart (`A2.contacts`, recorded: a contact is SP7b's reaction, not a settle defect).
- **A3** (:1530), per region: `lavaBelow54` the share of the tagged (cave) voxels at y < `aquifer.lavaLevel` (read from the profile; −50 by default; the layer the floor rule fills, so the level's own layer, which it leaves empty, is not counted: Spec correction, rulings R23 item 5) holding lava after settle, pooled over the group's voxels, ≥ 50 %, its population recorded beside it (`lavaBelow54Population`); an empty population reads NaN and is recorded as vacuous, not as a miss (the bound unchanged; Spec correction of D31, rulings R21 item 6: the part first counted the regions holding lava at y ≤ −55, where no region holds a tagged voxel); `aquiferLavaAbove10`, lava from aquifer cells (not from springs or settle) above y −10, 0 (by construction, gated from its commit); `lavaSupported`, lava voxels with a solid or lava voxel below, ≥ 99 % (settle's lava falls count as supported by the falling rule's definition: a falling lava voxel is fed from above); `lavaSkyRegions`, regions where lava touches sky-connected cave air (C2's components), ≥ 25 %. §6.5's traceability line ("lava aquifers + lava lakes → A3", :1609) becomes "lava aquifers, the bottom gullies and abyss lava springs (lava lakes SP8b)".
- **A4** `aquiferBodies` (:1531): 3D components of water voxels below the pre-cave top not connected to sky-open water, per region, ≥ 1, the minimum over regions.
- **A5** (new id, springs and falls; master row added):
  - `waterfalls`: visible water falls (§3.2: outlet open to the sky, straight fall ≥ 8) per km² of steep land (positions with `steep` ≥ 1.2 and `offset` ≥ 80), read on a fixed default-profile sample of seed '42' that holds steep land (Spec correction, rulings R20, Task 27b: the default regions' interiors hold 0.00056 km² of steep land, so one fall moved the reading by about 1,800 per km² and the part had no population): the chunks (cx, cz) = (4a, 4b), a and b in −240 … 239, whose column sample holds at least one steep position (chosen without reading the springs), their steep positions the steep land (1.055 km²: 10,809 chunks), the falls the sampled chunks' water springs from T (`terrainSpringsDebug`) whose outlet has no solid voxel above it and ≥ 8 non-solid voxels with no proto fluid below it (T's point probes; no settle: a lone spring's water falls there), pooled (`test/harness/waterfallSample.ts`); Point 2's bounds (the user's choice: 25-100, target about 50); recorded per quadrant of the lattice and the fall-height histogram; gated from the tuning on quick and full only (the sample is the same on every tier), in its own file, `test/metrics/waterfalls.metric.ts`, read in 4 worker threads (§14; Task 31). The caves regions' pooled reading stays recorded in `.out/A5.<group>.json`.
  - `lavaFalls`: lava springs with a fall ≥ 4 per 100,000 abyss cave-air voxels, in the biome region and every region with ≥ 50,000 abyss cave-air voxels; ≥ 1 (**estimate**, confirmed at D31). At the tuning the proposed 1 missed (the biome region's 3 falls, 0.647 on every tier; presets 0.887 on full), and the user set the bound **≥ 0.6** (rulings R24 item 4), gated in the groups that hold its sample, presets and biomes, on every tier (Task 31c).
  - `islandFalls`: island-edge falls per km² of island area in the floating_islands region; ≥ 2 (**estimate**).
  - `borderRatio`: springs whose voxel lies on a chunk-border voxel (x or z ∈ {0, 15}) over springs, divided by 60/256; 0.8-1.25, per caves group over every region of the group, gated in each group that holds a sample, the part the worst group's (the caves metric runs each group in its own CI job, and a job cannot read another job's regions: the plan's Spec correction 11). A sample is **≥ 50 springs** (the user, rulings R24 item 5: a sample-size rule, the bound unchanged; with 4 or 25 springs the ratio's standard error is about 0.9 or 0.36, and every reading of the dry run lay within 1.4 of them from 1): the presets group on full (78 springs, 0.875), so the row is gated on full only; the other groups and tiers record it, and a gated group holding fewer than 50 springs fails rather than passing (Task 31c).
  - `abyssWater`: the aquifer's water voxels whose nibble is the abyss: the voxels of the abyss nibble whose proto byte (T's) holds water, since T writes water at a tagged voxel only from the aquifer (springs and sky-open water are untagged, nibble 0); 0, by construction (the aquifer tests the voxel's own nibble, §1.6), gated from its commit. The settle's water there is `waterIntoAbyss` (Spec correction, rulings R23: the part counted every water voxel of the abyss nibble after settle, 0 while the settle never entered it).
  - `springsTruncated`: water springs whose settled fluid (the voxels fed from that spring, by its own front) holds an `unsettled` voxel outside the water/lava contacts (§4.1's merge sets bit 6 on a contact; a contact is SP7b's reaction, counted in `A2.contacts`, and A2's `unsettled` leaves it out too: the plan's Spec correction 19), over water springs, per caves group over the default group's regions and the presets group's amplified region (the mountain regions), gated in each group that holds a sample (a literal per group decided at the tuning's dry run), the part the worst group's (Spec correction 11); its bound is set at D31 from the reading and the shots (**proposed** ≤ 5 %, a ceiling, not a target), gated at the tuning. The aim is as close to 0 as the levers reach (the user, approving the spec: "evitar al máximo posible los cortes abruptos de las cascadas, si hay algún caso lo tendré que ver dentro del mundo para saber si rompe mucho la experiencia inmersiva o no"): at D31 and the tuning, every lever of §11 is applied in turn while it still lowers the reading without breaking another gate, and the bound is then set just above the value reached, never left at 5 % for comfort; the levers tried and what each gave go in the Threshold log. It is the user's "cortes abruptos" measured on the waterfalls themselves, which A2's share, diluted by the oceans' volume, cannot see. At the tuning (Task 31) the levers left it at 0.5 on quick and 0.684 on full (13 of 19 water springs in the full tier's four amplified regions), and its bound is the worst reading over the tiers it gates, rounded up to the next 0.1 %: ≤ 68.5 %, gated in the presets group (the default group's regions hold no spring on any tier) on quick and full (fast measures no preset region): a loosening of the proposed 5 % the user approved (rulings R23 item 1), each truncated spring checked by the user in the world (§13.9).
  - Recorded: `clarkEvans` of the visible waterfalls (proposed 0.7-1.5), `waterIntoAbyss` (the water the settle brought into the abyss: voxels of the abyss nibble holding settled water whose proto byte holds none, a spring's stream or a lake's spill that fell or spread into abyss air; Spec correction, rulings R23: it counted the water fronts stopped at abyss air, also in `C7.waterWall`'s population, while the abyss stop stood), `abyssWaterFace` (water voxels next to an abyss voxel holding air, §1.6), `adjacentSprings` (springs with another spring among their 6 neighbours, §3.1).
- **F1-F3** (:1557-1559) get `THRESHOLDS` rows with `activeFrom` 'SP7b' (recorded as inactive in SP7a). SP7a's share: F1's converged shapes (the radius-7 diamond, falls, infinite sources) as unit tests of the settle (§13.7), and F3's precondition (truncated fronts carry bit 6, so the sim can leave them alone), which A2 measures.

### 13.2 B2, B5, C7, B3, the interlocks

- **B2 (voxel):** `riverChannelWater` ≥ 85 % (`test/thresholds.ts:49`, `test/metrics/surface.metric.ts:271-289`) re-asserted with the sky-open rule (unchanged by design). No new B2 part (Q16). `dryPitsBelowSea` (`surface.metric.ts:246`) recorded before and after settle (its intent is now A1's and B4's).
- **B5** `lakeAirAdjacent` (:1518; never built, the SP6a spec :399): lake source voxels (sky-open water at Lw inside a lake mask) with a horizontally adjacent non-solid fluid-free voxel, read **on T** (before settle: the voxels a lake would spill into; after settle such a voxel holds the lake's spill unless a nearer drop lies on another side of the shore source, since the nearest-drop search skips the lake's own sources, §4.1 and rulings R21, abyss air included since rulings R23 withdrew the abyss stop (Spec correction; Task 25 found the search walking through them, which left breaches dry); the settled effect is what is measured after settle, `B5.lakeSpill` below), over T windows of 6 × 6 chunks around the first 4 (fast) / 16 (quick) / 32 (full) enabled lake cells of seed 42 (`lakes.ts:93-133`). Also recorded after settle: non-source water derived from lake sources outside the lake mask (`B5.lakeSpill`). Under Point 3's recommendation the bound is master's 0, but it does not hold by construction: water v0 left lake-edge walls (O87: a position whose own level is −∞ beside lake water), which are exactly rim breaches. So the part is recorded in its commit and gated at 0 at the tuning; a reading above 0 goes first to the lake rim's leaves (`lakes.*`, the rim's height over Lw), then to the user with the measurements. Under the alternative, redefined (Point 3). At the tuning it read 76 / 247 / 451 on fast / quick / full (`lakes.rimRise` stays 2, rulings R23 item 4), and the user chose to **record it only** (rulings R24 item 6; Spec correction): its intent, no wall of water at a lake rim, is carried by the settle's spill into the breaches (`B5.lakeSpill`).
- **`C7.waterWall`** (gated ≤ 3.3 per km² from SP6a, `test/thresholds.ts:130`; `test/harness/caveMetrics.ts:1540-1541`): re-read **after settle** (the reference's output) with a new population, the true walls: fluid voxels horizontally next to dry tagged air into which §4.1's rules would flow but for the cap, that is truncated fronts with a dry target (Spec correction, rulings R23: the population also held the water fronts stopped at abyss air while §4.1's abyss stop stood). Falling voxels and spill rims (the level-7 water and level-6 lava ends) are flow, not walls, and are excluded: today's count (`test/harness/caveMetrics.ts:1540-1549`, any fluid voxel next to dry tagged air) would count every underground waterfall and lava fall that D4 and D6 ask for (about 10 + 28 voxels per fall and spill), so 3.3 per km² would allow less than one per 10 km². Reading T would count every aquifer border instead, which is settle's input. The change of population lands in the commit that lands the reference, with a Threshold-log line; the old count is recorded apart (`C7.waterWallAny`). The bound stays 3.3; a reading above it after the levers (barriers, then `A5.springsTruncated`'s levers) goes to the user (a LOOSEN is the user's). After the levers it read 718.9 fast, 435.1 / 463.4 quick and 282.3 / 279.0 full per km² (default / archipelago), and the user made it **record-only in SP7a** (rulings R24 item 1, a LOOSEN: its row `activeFrom` 'SP7b', the bound 3.3 kept there; still computed and written to `.out/C7.tuning.json`, with `C7.waterWallAny` beside it): SP7b, the runtime sim, owns its final definition and bound (§17), and the user reviews the fronts in-world at Task 47 (§13.9's `wallFrontN` links, listed by the harness). R13/R19's known-red list is empty from Task 31c.
- **B3** `lushMin`, `dripstoneMin`, `abyssMin`, `windowAir` (`test/thresholds.ts:59-65`) re-asserted (dry cave air only; flooded caves leave the population, K1).
- **Re-asserted at their bounds:** C1-C9 (K4; populations re-checked: C counts tagged voxels, wet or dry, as before), C7's bottom parts and `bedrockExposed` (K3), S1, B4, T4 (K9-K11), L1, L2 (K7), R3 (K6: fluid voxels stay open in `connectivity15`), DT2 (K8), U1-U4 (K13). Three Spec corrections of populations, no bound moved (rulings R24 items 7-8, the user's; Task 31c), because D24 opens caves through the sea and river beds and the aquifer floods them:
  - **C9:** an opening (`nearestEntrance`'s) is a voxel of a C2 component face-adjacent to untagged air holding **no fluid**, and `entrancesMax` counts the C2 components holding one (an entrance a player walks into); a cave sky-connected only through the sea or a river's water is no entrance (it read 183.1 per km² on full, 343 of 576 components wet only). C2's `skyComponents` keeps every sky-connected component, as before.
  - **T4:** the floor under water is the **pre-cave sea bed**, the highest voxel solid or tagged (C's `preCaveTops` rule), not `OCEAN_FLOOR_WG` − 1, which read a cave's bottom where D24 opened it through the bed (`deepFloor` 7 on full).
  - **R3:** SP6b §12.4's poses, "tagged cave air at several depths and kinds", are tagged cave air with no fluid, and a region with **no dry candidate for a pose kind** takes a fluid-filled cave voxel for that kind (the eye under water; culling treats fluid as open, K6); a kind with a dry candidate keeps it, so only seed '2' `cave_heavy` (−16, −16)'s corner pose on full moves (its central chunks lie under the sea, 99.6 % of their cave voxels flooded).

### 13.4 DT1, DT2 and the settle reference

- **The global settle reference** (`test/harness/refs.ts`, `referenceSettle`): the same `fluidRules.ts`, over a whole region's blocks and fluid in one breadth-first fixed point, no window, no cap other than the distance cap; slow and plain (SP6a's `referenceCarve` pattern). The metrics run it in the region workers (the SP6b spec §13's L6), about 1-5 s per 32 × 32-chunk region (**estimate**).
- **`DT1.settleReference`:** on a 10 × 10-chunk window at seed 42 holding springs, a coast and a flooded cave border, the final fluid of the interior 6 × 6 columns decorated in D (each from its own 3 × 3) against the reference over the window; and on two more windows chosen by the harness, one holding a water/lava contact (an aquifer lava cell beside a flooded cell) and one where a stream crosses a pool the settle made; mismatches 0, gated from its commit as a measured part (§4.1's radius argument is the proof; a nonzero reading is a windowing bug, not a tuning).
- **`DT1.settleIsolated`:** one column decorated alone (its 3 × 3 generated to T for it) against the same column in the region; 0.
- **`DT1.light`** (`test/metrics/light.metric.ts:401-405`) already hashes final blocks, fluid, light and cave over threads and orders: re-asserted on a window with springs.
- **Golden** `sp7a.region.D.water`: the final fluid hash of the 6 × 6 interior above (§15.3).
- **DT2:** `probeBulk` with `terrainSolidAt` (D20; Spec correction 4); new `aquiferProbe`: `fluidAt` against T's stored fluid at non-solid voxels, tagged and untagged (spring voxels excluded; both rules are point functions, D19), 0, gated from its commit; `tagProbe` and `caveBiomeProbe` re-asserted (barriers clear tags: the probe's tag reads 0 where `barrierAt` holds).

### 13.8 The generator checkpoint (D31)

Local, after the aquifer, the springs, the settle, the reference and the A parts exist, before the tuning and the golden record. Measured: A1-A5, A2, `C7.waterWall`, B3's minima, A3's parts; `terrain.real`, `terrain.islands`, `aquifer.column`, `decorate.column` with visits; barrier voxels in the abyss margin, `A5.abyssWaterFace`, `A5.springsTruncated`, B5 on T, sky-open water voxels more than 12 below `top` (D19); screenshots of three waterfalls, an island fall, a lava fall, a flooded cave under the sea and a dry cave below sea level. **Rule:** if `terrain.real` p99 > 19 ms, §1.8's levers before anything else; if `A5.waterfalls` (on its steep-land sample, §13.1) is outside Point 2's bounds, `springs.water.tries`; if A1 < 40 %, `aquifer.barrierBand` 25 → 30, then the inland thresholds +0.1; if `A5.springsTruncated` exceeds the bound this checkpoint sets (proposed ≤ 5 %), `springs.water.minFall` 8 → 6 (shorter streams on the slope below), then yLo measured from the ground below the outlet instead of the surface estimate, then the user with the shots; if A2 or `C7.waterWall` misses, barriers first, then the same spring levers, then the user with the measurements (policy B is SP7b's); if `A5.abyssWaterFace` > 0, `aquifer.abyssMargin` up; if B5 on T > 0, the lake rim's leaves, then the user; if A3's first part misses (as re-read at D31: the share of the tagged voxels at y ≤ `aquifer.lavaLevel` holding lava; an empty population is vacuous, not a miss), `aquifer.lavaLevel` −54 → −52, then the user (the floor mask); if `B3.abyssMin` < 0.005 (lava pools taking abyss air), `aquifer.lavaThreshold` 0.3 → 0.4, re-checking A3. Every lever and reading goes to the Exit evidence.

### 13.9 Review links, the user's check and the production check (D16)

- **Links** (`docs/superpowers/specs/assets/sp7a/water.examples.json`, mirrored in `test/tools/uiSmoke.ts` as `SP7A_POSES`, each with its profile): found by the harness from the A5 and A1 passes at seed 42, not by hand: `waterfall1`-`waterfall3` (the three tallest visible falls within 2 km of spawn, eye 24 blocks out at the fall's mid-height, facing it), `islandFall` (floating_islands), `lavaFall` and `lavaPool` (the abyss, night vision suggested), `floodedUnderSea` (the eye in a flooded cave under the sea; opened with the X-ray on), `dryBelowSea` (a dry cave below 63 under land), `riverMouth`, `lake`, `coastFancy` (a coast at grazing view), `underwater` (the eye 6 blocks under the sea surface).
- **Truncated waterfalls** (the user's addition, §13.1): the harness lists every water spring counted by `A5.springsTruncated` within 2 km of spawn at seed 42 (and at least the five nearest when there are more), each as a link `truncated1`… with the eye 16 blocks out facing the truncated front, so the user can judge in the world whether it breaks the immersion. If there are none, the review says so.
- **Water-wall fronts** (rulings R24 item 1, the condition of `C7.waterWall`'s LOOSEN, §13.2; Spec correction, Task 31c's review): the caves run lists, per caves group at seed 42, the voxels `waterWallCount` counts (a truncated front with a dry target), each with its dry side, and writes the nearest to spawn into `.out/sp7a.examples.<group>.json` (`wallFronts`, rulings P22's per-group record). The links `wallFront1`… are the five nearest spawn over the merged groups with no two within 32 blocks of each other (so five links show five fronts; fewer when fewer exist, and the review says so), each with the eye 16 blocks out on its dry side, level with it, facing the front, and its region, profile and the region's `C7.waterWall` reading. The user visits each at Task 47 (the controller's preview, before `main`) to judge whether the walls of water break the immersion; the verdict goes to Exit evidence and to SP7b's definition and bound (§17), and a front the user asks to fix in SP7a is a finding before `main`. Task 51 copies them into `water.examples.json` with a shot each.
- **Shots** (`--shots`, RD 12): one per link, plus `review-spill-slope.png`, `review-xray-flooded.png`, `review-slice-aquifer.png`, `review-fast-vs-fancy.png`.

- **`?selftest=1` and Bun** at 68/68 (§15.3), Chrome by the smoke, Firefox by the user.
- **The user's check** (what the user looks at, from the links, in the user's own Firefox on production): waterfalls on mountains that keep flowing; an island's waterfall; lava falls and a lava pool in the abyss (dark around, lit near the lava); a flooded cave under the sea (and in the X-ray); a dry cave below sea level; a river mouth and a lake; Fancy water at a coast; under water, the fog and tint. The user judges whether the waterfalls are visible but not everywhere (Point 2), and visits each `truncated` link to judge whether the cut breaks the immersion; a cut the user rejects is a finding before main (the `wallFrontN` links are reviewed earlier, at Task 47).
- **The production check** (as SP6b's Task 60): on production, after the merge with the user's go-ahead; Chrome by the agent under §13.6's GPU conditions with the EC CPU-cap check: Step 0 saves an SP6b world with an edit inside a cave that will flood (before the merge), then each link, the X-ray, the slice, the Water toggle, swimming at `underwater`, the SP6b world opened with today's terrain (the edit kept, the pocket dry), `?selftest=1` and the IndexedDB contract; Firefox by the user, by hand, the same links and `?selftest=1`. Recorded in `assets/sp7a/production-check.json`.

## 15. Governance

### 15.1 The first commit: the split

- `src/core/ids.ts:10-13`, `:16-19`: `'SP7'` → `'SP7a' | 'SP7b'` (26 sub-projects; the header comment gains "SP7 was split on 2026-11-17 into SP7a (water in the generated world) and SP7b (fluids that move when you edit)"); `CURRENT_SP` 'SP7a' (`:28`).
- `test/harness/sp.ts:22` → `SP7a: ['SP6b', 'SP5a']`, `SP7b: ['SP7a', 'SP5c']`; `:24-25` → `SP8b: ['SP7a', 'SP8a']`, `SP8c: ['SP7a', 'SP5a']` (SP8b's lava lakes feed SP7a's settle; SP8c's ambience reads generated water; neither needs the sim); `:38` `STARTED_SPS` appends 'SP7a'.
- `test/arch/sp.test.ts` (the current SP, "25 sub-projects" → 26, the `SP_ORDER` literal, the SP8c parallel paths, "SP7 needs SP6b").
- `test/thresholds.ts:178` R5 → 'SP7b' (§13.3: ungated until the commit that reaches ≤ 0.1 moves it to 'SP7a').
- The master's §2.5 union, §10 headers and the critical path (§16 items 1-3), with every `spEntry('SP7', …)` and `spEntry('SP6b', 'SP7')` pin of `test/arch/masterSpec.test.ts` (:1300, :1927, :1939, :1981, :1988-1990, :2016, :2022-2023, :2541, :3115, :3141, :3145, :3170, :3174, :3178, :3229, :3253, :3256) naming the new ids, and the pins that read SP7 in other entries' text: :1316-1318 (SP8a "in parallel with SP5a-SP7"), :1324 (the SP8c header "(M; SP7, SP5a;"), :1337 ("schedules fluid ticks … (SP7)", → SP7b), :2042 (the quart-lattice caching "SP7-SP9", §16 item 6), :2049 (the bench route's "SP7 the flood", → SP7b) and :3278 (the Default receivers' "SP7 extra lava reactions → SP12", §16 item 3, → SP7b). The pins of text that later items reword move into the SP7a describe with those items' commits: :2188 (§3.8's "carvers shrink to nothing under water … each carved voxel asks `aquifer.fluidAt` (SP7; until then it is air)", §16 item 5), :2673 (§3.9's "lava may appear there (SP7)", §16 item 7), :2615 (rule 4's "SP7, with aquifers", §16 item 8) and :2042 above (§16 item 6) (the plan's Spec correction 2).

### 15.3 `GENERATOR_VERSION` and goldens (D33)

- **The generator push** (the aquifer in T, the wet roof narrowed, the springs, rule 4's branches with the migration, the settle and its golden, the tuning): 9 → 10 in its first output-changing commit; the terrain stage 5 → 6 with its `params` gaining 'aquifer' and 'springs' (`src/core/stage/registry.ts:20`; U4's `checkRegistry` fails otherwise), decorate 1 → 2, and the stage owning the column sample's `wetMask` (`shape` 2 → 3, D24; `registry.ts:16-25`); every generator commit records from the push's base (`git checkout <base> -- test/goldens.json`, then `npm run test:goldens`), so the push shows one bump (`test/harness/governance.ts:46-52`). Every new leaf lands in it (`sp1.params`). Work that changes no generated output (the rules module, the mesher, the materials, the fog, the X-ray, the slice, the UI) is pushed before or after at will.
- **Allowed changes:** `generatorVersion`; `sp1.params`; `sp3a.region.T.default`, `.large_biomes`; `sp3e.region.T.amplified`, `.archipelago`, `.floating_islands`; `sp6a.region.T.caves`; `sp6b.region.T.biomes`; `sp3c.surface.ops` (its columns go through the surface probe, which now runs the aquifer). **Expected unchanged:** every `sp2a.*`, `sp3e.column.point.*` (neither hashes `wetMask`), `sp3b.density.default` (its fixture, column (0, 0) of seed '42', is dry: its 49 lattice `wetMask` values are 0 under SP6a's rule and D24's, pinned in `test/unit/columnStage.test.ts`, so a move is a bug; the plan's Spec correction 8), `sp3b.density.ops`, `sp3a.registry`, `sp3c.registry`, `sp6a.carver.paths`, `sp6a.density.ops`, `sp6b.caveBiome.points`, `sp6b.registry`.
- **New keys (65 → 68)** in `src/metrics/sp7aGoldens.ts`: `sp7a.aquifer.points` (`fluidAt` and `barrierAt` at fixed 3D points, seed '42'), `sp7a.springs` (the springs of fixed chunks on the three profiles that place each kind), `sp7a.region.D.water` (§13.4).
- **The number:** 10 at the exit; 11 if the user's check asks for a retune.

### 15.4 Saved worlds and preset files (D34)

- `SCHEMA_VERSION` 5 → 6 in the generator push's first commit (before rule 4's branches change `DEFAULT_SURFACE_RULES`, so the SP5b fixture test, `test/unit/saves.test.ts:74-80`, stays green: the SP6b spec's Spec correction 3), with `MIGRATIONS[4]`: a stored `surface.rules` canonically equal to the default or floating_islands built-in tree at `GENERATOR_VERSION` 9 (hashes pinned over a new `test/fixtures/gv9-surface-trees.json`, exported at the push base, with its pin test) becomes SP7a's; an edited tree is kept (its world gets no clay or gravel under cave water); density trees are untouched (D24; under Point 3's alternative, `gv9-density-trees.json` and a density map join). Missing `aquifer.*` and `springs.*` leaves take the profile's values (`migrate.ts:128-133`).
- U4 adds `preset-v5.json` and `expected-v5.json`; `test/unit/presetFile.test.ts:190`'s "newer than this build" case moves to `schemaVersion: 7` and "(6)".
- The world list keeps "opens with today's terrain; your blocks stay" (`src/ui/worldsMenu.ts:104`): a world saved in SP6b opens with aquifers, springs and settle; its edits stay absolute (§9.3).

Master-spec amendments made with this spec" and "## 17. Notes handed to later SPs" (SP6b's pattern, MST:2474-2496). Pins reading SP7's text move to the new describe.

1. **§10: SP7 → SP7a and SP7b.** A split paragraph (SP6a §13 item 1's pattern: "SP7 was split on 2026-11-17 into SP7a and SP7b (the user: \"Estoy de acuerdo con la forma 1\")"). `**SP7a — Water in the generated world** (XL; SP6b, SP5a). Spec: \`2026-11-17-sp7a-water-design.md\`.` (or the size the user chooses): aquifers (water, lava, barriers, the abyss's water off and lava on, fast path); seas, rivers and lakes at their `surfaceWaterLevel`; water springs in mountain faces, island-edge springs and lava springs in the abyss, placed by T; the exact 3 × 3 settle with the distance cap, `FLUID_MODE` and the unsettled flag; the flowing water and lava mesher; water tiers Fast and Fancy; underwater fog and tint; rule 4's under-water branches; the X-ray and slice showing flooded caves. **Deliverable:** "waterfalls from springs on mountains and island edges; translucent oceans, rivers and elevated lakes; flooded caves under the sea, dry caves below sea level under land; deep lava and the abyss's lava falls". The "Received from SP5c" line moves to SP7b; the "Received from SP6a" and "Received from SP6b" lines stay with SP7a, each item worded as delivered (or "→ SP10" for the slice's aquifer level and status field and the crosshair probe, "not needed" for the level-field rule, "→ SP9" for `surfaceEst3` caching, "the bench's flood scenario → SP7b"); a "Received from SP8b (moved by SP7a)" line: springs and island-edge springs feeding settle. **Exit:** "as the SP7a spec §19 (A1-A5; A2 after settle; B2 voxel; B5 with its third part; R5; DT1 with settle; P1 with the gates in force and the aquifer row; the Chrome legs with the water segment; CI ≤ 6 min on cold runs; the user's check)". **Cut line:** §18's wording; "vertex waves (→ SP11)". `**SP7b — Fluids that move when you edit** (size set by the SP7b spec; SP7a, SP5c)`: the runtime fluid sim (edit-triggered, 20 TPS, rules from `fluidRules.ts`, reactions obsidian, cobblestone and stone, caps, sim distance, diffs and the trailer's ticks); SP5c's paste, cut and move ticks and the 8k cap; the ghost drawing fluids; the bench's flood scenario; the current's push. **Deliverable:** "break a lake rim and watch it drain".
