## 17. Notes handed to later SPs

- **SP7b:** the runtime sim on `fluidRules.ts` (its single step is §4.1's); drains (unsupported flow); reactions (obsidian, cobblestone, stone; `obsidian` appended as a state); the caps, sim distance, the trailer's ticks (`src/persist/diffCodec.ts:4-6`, `:15`), coalescing and the remesh throttle; SP5c's paste, cut and move ticks and the 8k cap; the ghost drawing fluids; the bench's flood scenario (L15) and F1's tick log, F2, F3; truncated fronts wake only on an edit's update (bit 6 in the final set); the current's push in the walk controller; buckets placing flowing water; master's policy B if SP7a's A2 needed it; a pasted generated waterfall then flows; `C7.waterWall`'s final definition and bound (recorded, not gated, in SP7a, its row `activeFrom` 'SP7b': the user, rulings R24 item 1; it moved here from SP12's note), with the fronts the user reviews in-world at SP7a's Task 47 (§13.9's `wallFrontN` links) and the user's verdict on them (SP7a's Exit evidence).
- **SP8a:** water and lava animation frames (Fancy's animated texture); lava's emissive flag implied by its layer; the water tint per biome (`tint` bits, master §4.6).
- **SP8b:** lava lakes as a D step-1 feature feeding settle (§4.2's step 1); freeze_top_layer's ice on sky-exposed water (rule 5, S3's ice parts); the generated-value layer extended from fluid to blocks (D23); plants with `hold`/`displace` in settle (already read from the table); seagrass and kelp in flooded caves; floors under settle's spills re-skinned if wanted (§5); the decorate-scope Apply path (springs stay Terrain scope).
- **SP8c:** waterfall and lava-fall emitters (the spring list per column, which T can expose in aux B); underwater muffling from `eyeFluid`; AU2's waterfall and lava scenes on SP7a's links.
- **SP9:** structures and aquifers (a piece's box keeps its voxels dry: barriers or a structure term); `surfaceEst3`'s quart cache if structures need it.
- **SP10:** the crosshair probe's aquifer cell, level and status; the slice's "aquifer level and status" field; a map aquifer layer; F3's fluid byte line (if SP5a's HUD lacks it).
- **SP11:** vertex waves on near water; Fabulous water; the water tier in the quality presets (Low Fast); RegionMesh's per-section translucent sort if the Firefox waterfall look needs it (§6.6).
- **SP12:** water in mountain gorges (elevated rivers; a proposal, not asked for); the volcano's crater lava on SP7a's generated lava; extra lava reactions (via SP7b).

## 18. Cut line

May slip, in this order, each named in master §10's Default receivers and worded at the exit by whether it was taken:
1. **The X-ray's fluid tint** (and its legend rows, `R1.xrayFluid`) → SP10. Flooded caves still show as caves in the X-ray.
2. **The slice field's aquifer fluid and hover lines** → SP10 (with the field and probe already handed there). The blocks field and the Voxels mode still show aquifer water.
3. **The walk's fluid-height rule** → SP7b (with the current's push). Walking keeps SP5a's rule.
4. **The bench's water segment** (Fancy's 1.2 ms measured) → SP11.

Each is cut whole. The plan builds all four unless the controller cuts one, or the user takes Point 1's trimmed scope (which also moves Fancy and the Water setting to SP11).

Not cuttable (the brief and master §10): the aquifer with barriers, lava and the abyss rule; the sky-open rule; caves under the sea and rivers; the three spring kinds; the settle with its machinery and the generated-fluid layer; the flowing water and lava mesher; underwater fog; rule 4's branches; Fancy (unless Point 1 (a)); A1-A5, A2, B5, R5, DT1 with settle, P1; the migration; the Chrome legs.

## 19. Exit criteria

| criterion | checked by |
|---|---|
| A1 ≥ 40 %; A2 ≤ 0.1 % / ≤ 0.1 %; A3's four parts; A4 ≥ 1 per region | `npm run test:metrics:full`, CI |
| waterfalls visible but not everywhere: `A5.waterfalls` within Point 2's bounds; `lavaFalls`, `islandFalls`; no grid: `A5.borderRatio`, `clarkEvans` recorded; the waterfall shots | metrics, review |
| the abyss dry: `A5.abyssWater` 0; lava in it (`lavaFalls`, the shots) | metrics, review |
| no water walls, no abrupt stops and no seams: `C7.waterWall` after settle (its new population) at its bound (or the user's, logged); `A5.springsTruncated` within the bound set at D31; `DT1.settleReference` (with the contact and stream-over-pool windows), `settleIsolated` 0 | metrics |
| B2's `riverChannelWater` ≥ 85 %; B5 with `lakeAirAdjacent` 0 on T (or the user's, logged) | metrics |
| R5 ≤ 0.1 on the named water regions; R1 0 with flowing water and lava; `R1.xrayFluid` 0 | metrics |
| B3, C1-C9 (C7's bottom parts and `bedrockExposed` 0), S1, B4, T4, L1, L2, R3, DT1, DT2 (`probeBulk` with barriers, `aquiferProbe` 0) within their bounds | metrics |
| P1: `terrain.real` ≤ 13 / 20 ms, `terrain.islands` ≤ 14.5 / 26.5 ms (or Point 4's alternative); `aquifer.column` ≤ 1.0 / 2.5 ms; `light.column.lava` ≤ 4 / 8 ms; `slice.caves128` ≤ 300 ms; `decorate.column` and its visits recorded; every other row within its gate and +30 % | `npm run bench` |
| D31's checkpoint run before the tuning, its readings and levers recorded | Exit evidence |
| no stutter: every G1, G1.enter, G2 cell re-measured into `assets/sp7a/` and within its gate; L13 on dry air; the water segment (`waterFancyGpuMs` ≤ 1.2, `underwaterP95` ≤ 16.7); Firefox recorded (the user's run) | `g1.metric.ts`, `assets/sp7a/*.json` |
| the world changed only as allowed: `GENERATOR_VERSION` 10 (11 after a retune); the golden diff against the push's base lists only §15.3's keys; `?selftest=1` 68/68 in Chrome and Firefox; Bun 68/68 | `git diff <base> -- test/goldens.json`, browsers, Bun |
| worlds saved in SP6b open with aquifers, springs and rule 4's branches (D34); an SP6b world opened in production | `npm test`; the production check |
| the smoke's water steps green | `node test/tools/uiSmoke.ts` |
| build, tests and full metrics green with SP7a's rows active | `npm run build && npm test && npm run test:metrics:full` |
| CI ≤ 6 min 0 s on the last two cold runs before the exit commit, 20 jobs; each measured run's job table recorded | GitHub Actions |
| production, headful: Chrome by the agent under §13.6's conditions with the EC CPU-cap check; Firefox by the user | §13.9 |
| the review links and shots in `assets/sp7a/` | Exit evidence; `uiSmoke.ts --shots` |
| the master amendments pinned in 'master spec amendments (SP7a §16)'; every obligation of §20 placed | `npm test` (arch); §20 |
| **the user's check:** waterfalls on mountains and an island that keep flowing, the abyss's lava falls, a flooded cave under the sea, a dry cave below sea level, a river and a lake, Fancy water and the underwater fog (§13.9) | the user's evaluation |

The exit commit says in `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` that SP7a is implemented and awaits CI, the production check and the user's check; the commit after them sets SP7a complete ("docs: SP7a complete").

## Exit evidence

(Filled during and at the exit: the first commit's split; D31's checkpoint — A1-A5, A2, `C7.waterWall`, B3's minima, `terrain.real` and `terrain.islands`, `aquifer.column`, `decorate.column` and its visits, the screenshots, the levers applied; R5's measuring task and levers; the golden diff of the generator push against its base; the bench; the Chrome legs and the user's Firefox run; the review links and screenshots; CI's runs; the production check; the user's check.)

### The abyss margin (Task 6)

§1.6's m, measured on seed '42''s default profile (2026-11-18, a scratch script, not committed). Columns drawn uniformly from cx, cz in −4,000 … 3,999 (a fixed `Xoshiro128` stream) until 4,098 positions were found on the abyss box's unjittered sides, at most 4 per column (91,728 columns drawn, 11,793 of them holding a position inside the box): positions (x, z) of the column's 16 × 16 where, between (x, z) and (x + 1, z) or (x, z + 1), E′ crosses `abyss.eMax` with C′ > `cMin` (4,077 positions) or C′ crosses `cMin` with E′ ≤ `eMax` (21), and the box's depth is reached above the rock floor, (Y0 + 47)/`depthScale` ≥ `depthMin` (SP6b's `BIOME_SCAN_ROOM_Y` −47). The gradient across the side is |∇E′| (or |∇C′|) per block by central differences over the cave-biome column pass. Its 50th percentile 0.00388, **95th 0.00956**, 99th 0.01263, maximum 0.01746 (95th on E′ sides 0.00949, on C′ sides 0.01730). m = 6 × 0.00956 = 0.0573, rounded up to 0.005: **m = 0.06**, a lateral margin of 6.28 blocks at the 95th percentile. The default the tests use (`test/harness/aquiferDefaults.ts`) and Task 13's leaf.

### The re-asserted parts (Task 25)

B5's lake rim on T and its settled spill, and §13.2's re-asserted parts, measured 2026-11-22 on Task 25's tree over becc5f4 (quick one metric file at a time, `METRICS_PROFILES=default` for `surface.metric.ts` and `terrain.metric.ts`, each caves group alone; fast in `npm test`).

**B5** (recorded in `.out/B5.lake.json`, gated at the tuning, Task 31; `test/harness/water.ts`' `lakeAirAdjacentCount` and `lakeSpillCount`, read by `region.metric.ts`). The windows are 6 × 6 chunks around the chunk of each enabled lake cell's centre, the cells taken in the square spiral from cell (0, 0) (`liveness.ts`' order) at seed '42', profile default; the lake mask is the positions whose nearest quart corner holds a finite `lakeLevel`. The centre lies in warped lake space and the default lake spans about 90-450 blocks, so a window holds its lake whole, a part with its shore, or none of it. Every lake source the definition finds lies at its cell's Lw (a scratch check over fast's four windows: 19,200 sources, none off Lw).

| B5 | fast (4 windows) | quick (16 windows) |
|---|---|---|
| `lakeAirAdjacent` on T (lake sources at Lw beside a passable dry voxel) | 76 | 247 |
| `B5.lakeSpill` after settle (non-source water outside the mask fed by the lake) | 643 | 2,035 |
| windows with a shore / lake positions / shore positions | 4 / 20,088 / 378 | 12 / 39,746 / 1,338 |
| per window, `lakeAirAdjacent` (`lakeSpill`) | 5 (89), 23 (436), 34 (112), 14 (6) | fast's four, then 11 (38), 0, 16 (26), 0, 27 (9), 26 (18), 0, 0, 0 (0; 1,539 lake positions), 91 (1,301), 0, 0 |
| seconds (T, reference) | 3.8 (1.5, 1.8) | 13.6 (6.2, 6.5) |

So `lakeAirAdjacent` is not 0 on T (master's bound, §13.2): lake water stands beside dry air at the rims of 9 of the 12 quick windows that hold a shore. Task 27's B5 lever (the lake rim's leaves, then the user) reads it. The reading after settle is not vacuous either, contrary to §13.2's remark. A source spreads only toward its nearest drop, and the lake's own voxels at Lw over its water are drops at distance 1. So a shore source spills into a breach only when the breach offers a drop at the same distance, or the lake is 1 deep for 4 blocks along the shore. Water that lands below a breach beside the lake finds the lake's voxels as its nearest drop and spreads no further. `test/unit/water.test.ts` pins three cases: a notch over rock stays dry after settle; a pit beside the shore takes only the fall; a 1-deep shelf spills along the notch, down the fall and over the floor below. Part of each breach is therefore a wall after settle as well: the visible case of `lakeAirAdjacent`. (Superseded by Task 27k: the search now skips the fluid's own sources, rulings R21, and those pins were rewritten there; see "Task 27k: the nearest-drop rule" below.)

### D31's generator checkpoint (Task 26)

§13.8's checkpoint, measured 2026-11-22/23 on Task 26's tree over 8897a29: the generator as Tasks 13-21 left it (the aquifer, the lake roof, the settle in D, the three spring kinds), the metrics of Tasks 22-25. The bench gains its two rows (§13.5, D32): `aquifer.column`, T's aquifer step alone (`aquiferColumn` on `aquiferOf`, the phases before it run untimed through `terrainAquiferDebug`, whose inputs give T's own fluid, checked on the first column) over `terrain.real`'s first 256 candidate columns, each timed once, gated at `AQUIFER_P50_MAX_MS` 1.0 / `AQUIFER_P99_MAX_MS` 2.5; `decorate.column`, D with its settle (reopened at Proto, never committed) on the 256 interior columns of `light.column`'s region and the 36 of `SETTLE_WINDOWS[0]`, recorded with the settle's visits (`decorateStatsInto`). The reference machine: the EC check before each bench session (AC online; 6 own busy threads at 4.1 GHz, 20 at 4.1 GHz settling to 2.6-3.1 GHz under PL1, so no cap), the 1-minute load ≤ 1.5 at each run's start, `BENCH_EXEMPT=terrain.real,terrain.islands,surface.column npm run bench` twice; the metrics on quick, one file at a time (each caves group alone, `terrain.metric.ts` with `METRICS_PROFILES=default`), read once: they are deterministic and equal Tasks 22-25's readings to the last digit (no generated block changed since Task 21), so the run 2 column holds fast's reading (`npm test`) where it differs.

`terrain.real`'s p99 over 83-84 columns is the worst or second-worst column's time: one column near 21.8-22.7 ms decides it, so it lands on either side of 20 ms from run to run (four runs today: 19.972, 21.770, 19.892 at the record, 22.732 in the gated run after it), and its p50 sits 1.2-1.4 ms above §1.8's estimate. `aquifer.column` stays within its own gate (p50 0.41 ms, p99 margin about 0.46 ms), so most of T's p50 rise (9.89 ms in SP6b's baselines to about 11.2) lies outside the step itself (the springs, the seed-section scan and the rest of T's SP7a work, not measured apart here). Task 27a's levers are §1.8's, measured on both rows.

**The screenshots** (§13.8, §13.9's poses; RD 12, headless Chrome on SwiftShader, 1400 × 900, seed '42'; the scratch script `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-17-sp7a-water/shots.mjs`, rulings P37, with an aimed mode: from each candidate the eye moves to the nearest clear line of sight at the wanted distance; not committed). The waterfall candidates are the A5 passes' (`.out/sp7a.examples.<group>.json`, merged): one in amplified and the biome region's, which is also the fourth truncated spring, so the second and third waterfall come from a scratch scan of the default profile around spawn (the two nearest water springs with a fall ≥ 8 that it found, outside the caves regions, falls of 15). The truncated springs are every one of the merged candidates: three within 2 km of spawn (amplified) and the biome region's at 2,112 blocks, so four, fewer than five (no other candidate exists in the regions).

None of the twelve poses shows a wall of water. Two of the four truncated springs show a hanging end in view (1 and 3), one a mild one (2), one barely (4): Task 28 starts from these. SwiftShader draws a faint vertical seam down the middle of some frames (a shading step between the two halves); it is the software rasterizer's, not the scene's.

**Step 3, the rule** (D31): fired, in Task 27's order: 27a (`terrain.real` p99 > 19 ms, and its 20 ms gate missed in two of the four runs), 27b (`A5.waterfalls` 0 in the default group), 27d (`C7.waterWall`, R19), 27e (`A5.abyssWaterFace` 1,719 and 43), 27f (B5 247 on T), 27g (`A3.lavaBelow54` 0), 27j (C5's fast `thinnestByKind` 1.444, R13); then Task 28 (`A5.springsTruncated` 0.6). Not fired: 27c (A1), 27h (B3), 27i (D). Missed with no lever named by §13.8: `A5.lavaFalls` in the biomes job (0.645) and `A5.borderRatio` in both groups with a sample (0.186, 2.133), recorded for Tasks 27-28 and the tuning. Task 26 runs no lever (the controller's dispatch: the evidence is committed as measured).

**Step 4, the record**: after the EC check (as above), `npm run bench:record` wrote `test/baselines.json` (its absolute gates passed in that run: `terrain.real` 11.196 / 19.892 ms, `aquifer.column` 0.408 / 2.017 ms, `decorate.column` 1.144 / 1.640 ms; ratios against the old baselines `terrain.real` ×1.13, `terrain.islands` ×1.11, `light.column` ×1.19, `terrain.caves.warm` ×1.09, the new rows added); the gated `npm run bench` right after it failed on `terrain.real` p99 22.732 ms > 20 ms alone (every ratio within +30 %, every other absolute gate met). Task 27a re-records after its levers.

**Task 27a, T's cost** (§1.8's levers in order, output-keeping; rulings R20; Task 6's fast-equals-slow test re-run after each; no golden moved). The EC check before the bench session (AC online; 6 own busy threads at 4.1 GHz, 20 settling to 2.6-3.1 GHz; no cap); the 1-minute load below 1.5 at each run's start but run 1's (4.9, right after the check). A profile of `terrain.real`'s sequence (its first 160 columns, phases timed apart, scratch) shows where the worst columns spend: the slowest in the timed window (column 21 of the sequence, 20.4 ms) 7.6 ms in the density fill, 7.2 ms in the tag and the cave-biome step, 2.26 ms in the aquifer step and 0.44 ms in the seed-section scan; so the aquifer is the only §1.8 term in the worst columns, and SP6's fill and tag (unchanged by SP7a) set most of their time.
- **Lever 1, the status cache per region instead of per job: not taken** (no commit). `terrain.real`'s and `aquifer.column`'s columns lie 7,919 and 104,729 chunk strides apart, so a cache shared across a worker's columns never hits on the gated rows: it cannot move the reading. What it would save while streaming is bounded by the step's status work per column: its 9 `samplePoint` calls (0.045 ms, measured) and about 100 cell statuses (Nf, Nspread, Nlava: a few hundredths of a ms), about 0.1 ms of a column's 11.
- **Lever 2, skipping voxels whose 4 nearest cells are all empty and non-lava: taken**, in its general form: where a voxel's 12 candidates share one status (all empty and non-lava being one case) its two nearest hold that status whatever their distances, so the step reads no distance there (s1 = s2: no barrier; the status's fluid below its level, the margin and the −54 rule as before); each neighbourhood also lists its 12 cache entries once, so the voxels that do read distances skip the per-candidate cache lookups (rulings R11 (b)'s hoisting). Output-keeping by construction and by test: `aquiferColumnSlow` (unchanged) equals the step byte for byte on Task 6's 48 columns × 9 settings and on `aquifer.column`'s 256 columns; the golden record from `<push base>` equals HEAD's. `aquifer.column` p50 0.41 → 0.30 ms, p99 2.0 → 1.0 ms; `terrain.real` p99 18.7-19.9 ms in five gated runs (was 19.9-22.7 in four), p50 10.9-11.1 ms.
- **Lever 3, the barrier noise only within the d2 − d1 band: already built** (Task 6: `ruleOf` reads Nb only when s1 ≠ s2, d2 − d1 < `barrierBand` and −2 ≤ p ≤ 2), so there is nothing left to take.
- **Result:** `terrain.real` p99 sits at 19 ± 0.6 ms (one or two of 85-86 columns decide it), inside its 20 ms gate in every run but not ≤ 19 ms in three consecutive runs: §13.8's trigger stays missed after §1.8's levers, a STOP for the user with these readings (the remaining time is SP6's fill and tag, outside §1.8; the seed-section scan, 0.07-0.44 ms against §1.8's 0.05-0.1 estimate, is the next SP7a term).

**Task 27b, the waterfall count** (rulings R20). `A5.waterfalls` as §13.1 first worded it had no population in the gated default regions (0.00056 km² of steep land in six interiors; the amplified region 0.0434 km², the biome region 0.0249), so it is read on a steep-land sample instead (§13.1 reworded; master §6.4's A5 row likewise): a fixed-stride scan of the chunk lattice (stride 4 chunks, 480 × 480 lattice chunks around the origin, ±15.4 km) keeping the chunks whose column sample holds steep land (`steep` ≥ 1.2 and `offset` ≥ 80; 4.7 % of the lattice), 10,809 chunks holding 1,054,945 steep positions (1.055 km², above R20's 1 km²; the regions' ground-top test is not read, `offset` ≥ 80 standing for it). The falls come from T and its probes (no settle), §3.2's definition: each sampled chunk's water springs (`terrainSpringsDebug`) whose outlet has no solid voxel above it up to y 319 (`terrainSolidAt`) and at least 8 non-solid voxels with no proto fluid below it (`fluidAt`); at `minFall` 8 every water spring's fall is at least 8 by the common rule, so the count is the springs whose outlet is open to the sky (32 of 64; the others lie under an overhang). The reading, 30.3 per km² (fall heights 8-42, median 16), is inside Point 2's 25-100, so 27b's lever does not fire (rulings R4); a scratch run at `springs.water.tries` 5, the lever's first step, reads 56.9 per km² (60 of 111), recorded for the user, who may prefer it as closer to the 50 asked for (`terrain.real` not measured at 5). Cost: the scan 72 s and the springs 146 s on one thread (`test/harness/waterfallSample.ts`; how the tuning reads it in CI is Task 31's).

**Task 27d, `C7.waterWall`** (rulings R19, R20: barriers first, 27c's first step). `aquifer.barrierBand` 25 → 30 changes the generated world (the golden record from `<push base>` moves exactly Task 21's keys) and thickens the barriers between differing cells: on quick the settled walls fall in both gated groups (default 446.8 → 370.4, archipelago 353.8 → 274.0 per km²) and A1, A2, A3, A4, C1-C9 stay within their gates; on fast (`npm test`, two regions) `C7.waterWall` rises 529.0 → 596.8 while C5's `thinnestByKind` turns green (1.444 → 1.556). R20 keeps the step only if `C7.waterWall` falls and no other gate goes red; it does not fall on every tier it gates, and R19 treats a fast reading above 529.0 as a finding, so the step is reverted and recorded. Either way the part stays two orders of magnitude above its 3.3 per km² bound: barriers alone cannot reach it. STOP for the user: take the band 30 anyway (quick −17 to −23 %, C5 fast green, fast C7 +13 %), or leave 25; then Task 28's spring levers, then policy B (the sim, SP7b, O80).

**Task 27e, water at the abyss.** `A5.abyssWaterFace` (water voxels beside abyss air) reads 1,719 in the presets job and 43 in the biomes job whatever the margin: at m 0.07, 0.08 and the leaf's maximum 0.20 the margin turns more water into rock (presets' margin barriers 35,641 → 63,623 at 0.20) but not one face moves, and `waterIntoAbyss` (settled fronts stopped at abyss air) stays 1,118 / 3. The faces are therefore not the aquifer water §1.6's margin governs (a tagged voxel the aquifer would fill inside the widened box) but water the margin never sees: 1,118 of the presets job's 1,719 are the settle's fronts stopped at abyss air (`waterIntoAbyss`; §4.1: the settle never enters the abyss, so a front reaching it stops there with bit 6), the rest not classified here (sky-open or spring water against abyss air, or falls over it, are the candidates). No margin value reaches 0, so nothing is committed (m stays 0.06) and the reading goes to the user; a lever that would reach it acts on the settle's fronts or on the abyss's own air (out of §13.8's list).

### Task 27k: the nearest-drop rule

§4.1's nearest-drop search corrected (rulings R21 item 8, the user's "Ok a todo"; Task 25's finding, Concern 1): the breadth-first search never enters, and never takes as a first side, a voxel holding a proto source of the fluid's own type (Minecraft's slope search, **MC (memory)**); the drop test (a searched voxel whose voxel below is open for the type, its own proto fluid there included), the targets when no drop is in reach (every open side), the caps and every other rule are unchanged. `fluidRules.ts` exports the search over the proto as `nearestDropDirsFor(type, r: FluidRuleReader, x, y, z)`, the one `ruleOffers` uses, and C7.waterWall's `spreadSides` (`test/harness/caveMetrics.ts`) now reads it, so the wall population agrees with the settle; `nearestDropDirs` keeps its blocks-only form (a `PassableReader` holds no proto fluid). ### Task 27n: water into the abyss (rulings R23 item 3)

The user's choice of 2026-11-23 (R23 item 3): settled water may enter abyss air under §4.1's ordinary rules; the aquifer still writes none there (§1.6). `ruleOffers` drops its three abyss tests (the fall, the spread targets and the new-source target), so water's offers into nibble-3 air are those into any air, and the cap flag (+16) is set there as anywhere; the nearest-drop search already searched abyss air (rulings R7). No rule reads the nibble any more, so §4.1's radius (cap + search ≤ 16) is unchanged. ### Task 31p: terrain.real p99 (rulings R24 item 2)

A perf task before Task 32, output-keeping (no parameter, no gate and no voxel moved). Base 2259e5a (Task 31c's head).

**Profile** (scratch, `fillColumnT` over `terrain.real`'s candidate sequence t = 1 … 120, each column timed 3 times, the minimum kept; the phases timed apart by a scratch copy of T's sequence). p50 10.9 / p99 18.6 ms; the p99 is one or two columns. The worst five, chunk (x, z), total and split (ms): t 8 (−26648, 27832) 21.6: density fill 9.97, carve 1.38, tag 5.35, cave biome 0.74, aquifer 0.78, surface 0.98, springs 1.39, seed sections 0.28; t 102 (−2262, −27642) 21.3: 11.25 / 1.49 / 5.16 / 0.78 / 0.44 / 1.21 / 0.42 / 0.08; t 99 (−26019, 18171) 20.3: 10.02 / 2.16 / 5.30 / 0.89 / 0.58 / 0.76 / 0.02 / 0.07; t 35 (7165, −24485) 20.1: 11.55 / 1.53 / 3.04 / 0.37 / 0.40 / 2.03 / 0.35 / 0.08; t 18 (−7458, −4878) 19.7: 8.85 / 1.44 / 4.70 / 0.82 / 1.54 / 1.66 / 0.03 / 0.29. The mean over the 120: density 6.49, carve 1.52, tag 1.50, surface 1.34, aquifer 0.41, cave biome 0.39, springs 0.22, seed sections 0.16. A CPU profile of the worst ten (`node --cpu-prof`): the fill 49 % (the interval program 21 %, `voxelFn` 16 %, corners 12 %), the tag 21 % (`tagVoxel` 17 %), the carve 7 % (path building 6 %), the surface pass 7 %. So SP6's fill, tag and carve set the worst columns; SP7a's own steps are about 2 ms of 21. Two facts made the tag and the carve cheaper without moving a voxel: 35-50 % of the worst columns' `tagVoxel` calls (4,200-4,800 of 9,000-13,000) were at voxels the fill had just evaluated, whose taps `voxelFn` had memoised, and each tagged cell's corner interval was computed twice (fill and tag); and in these far-apart columns about 1.8 ms per column went to building carver paths that miss the column (118 of the window's 121 built paths per column).

**Commits** (each `npm run typecheck && npm test` green; each proved by tests of its own and an unchanged 305-column region hash of T's output, scratch):
- 23b371a `perf(caves)`: the tag pass's cell kinds and voxel kinds recorded during the density fill (`FillHooks` on `fillDensityColumn`, `PreTags` in caves/tags.ts; test: with and without them, the fill and every tag of the 64 bit-exact columns and of the muted trees are equal).
- 0e73461 `perf(caves)`: partial cell tags (`cellKnown`: in a cell its intervals leave undecided, the taps they already decide at every voxel are not read; covered by the cell-tags on/off and muted-tree tests).
- a86d221 `perf(caves)`: the carve builds no path that cannot reach its column (`carverMayReach`, the cache's `pathNear`; test: a ruled-out source has no ellipsoid meeting the column, over 4 windows at the defaults with every roll passed and at the leaves' maxima).
Not taken (measured, slower): splitting an undecided cell into boxes for the tag (`boxInterval` per box cost more than it saved at every split size, 16 to 64 voxels).

**Output**: the goldens re-recorded on HEAD (rulings R8's split form; caves.metric SIGKILLed at 6 workers and re-run at 3 into the same obs dir, then merged): `git diff --exit-code HEAD -- test/goldens.json` empty, and no commit of the task touches the file (`git diff 2259e5a -- test/goldens.json` empty); every metric of the quick tier passed in that run.

**Measured** (scratch, both trees in one process, columns interleaved A/B so clock changes hit both alike; per column the median of 6, over t = 1 … 100): base → HEAD p50 10.71 → 9.57 ms, p99 20.54 → 18.33 ms, the sum ×0.887; a second round 13.47 → 12.16 / 27.10 → 23.09 (×0.888; the clocks capped, below). The worst columns lose 2.0-4.4 ms.

**The bench protocol (R22)**, run by Task 31q on HEAD d75ecef (Task 31p's code) after the user's EC reset. At Task 31p the EC check read the CPU capped three times (22:26-22:30: 20 own busy threads 1.34-1.50 GHz at 53-55 °C), so nothing was measured until the reset. ### R5's measuring task (Task 33)

R5 (§6.5, D27) now reads water quads on top faces of surface voxels (water with no water above) over the distinct surface top faces, on the Medium meshing, over the interior columns of its named water regions (`test/metrics/render.metric.ts`, the count `surfaceTopWaterCounts` in `test/harness/water.ts`, rulings P34). The water windows were found by `scanWaterWindow` (`test/harness/windowScans.ts`, kept as code with the other window scans, rulings P18/P28's precedent) at seed '42', profile default, and pinned: fast's `R5_COAST_FAST` V(−15, −15) of 4 × 4 chunks (ring 15: 890 sea and 2,137 land positions, 276 flowing water voxels); quick's and full's `R5_COAST` V(−8, −8) of 8 × 8 (ring 8: 13,118 sea, 2,158 land, 839 flowing) and `R5_LAKE` V(−2, 0) of 8 × 8 (ring 2: 3,413 lake positions). Quick and full also read R1's regions, which gain the interiors of `SETTLE_WINDOWS[0]` and `[1]` (the coast with its spills and spring, the water/lava contact with flowing lava; rulings P42) on every tier. R2 and R4 keep their regions (R2 quick reads 631,194 visible opaque faces, as before). Light comes next (0.2386, −0.050). `cornerDrop` changes nothing: flat tops share their drops (a still body sits at drop 1), and a sloped top is one quad per voxel by §6.2's rule whatever its key. That rule is the `cornerDrop` part, and the sloped share of the tops (0.334 on full) measures it. Removing all of `depth` would still leave 0.175 > 0.1, so Task 34's levers start from these readings.

So no change to key A or key B can reach 0.1 while sloped tops stay one quad per voxel.

Before Lever 1, the shallow shelf shades smoothly from light to dark through 15 steps. After it, the shelf shows four flat bands with hard edges at the band boundaries. The strongest edge is 4-7 → 8-15, where the colour jumps from 6/15 to 12/15 of the way between shallow and deep and draws a sharp contour line around the deep water. Open sea deeper than 15 is a little lighter than before (12 in place of 15). Fancy's Fresnel and ripples soften the steps, but they stay visible. Verdict: the step is visible on both tiers. The user judges it in-world at Task 47.

### The bench's water rows (Task 42)

§13.5's last two P1 rows (D14, D32), on f36c9b9 (Task 41's head); no generated block changes (no golden diff).

- **`light.column.lava`** (gated at `light.column`'s `LIGHT_P50_MAX_MS` 4 / `LIGHT_P99_MAX_MS` 8, `test/bench/gates.ts`, `BENCH_ROWS` right after `light.column`): `light.column`'s job and protocol (`LIGHT_RUN`, `mayEmit` false, each column timed once) on 256 columns whose light region (`copyLightRegion`'s copy) holds generated lava, so `lightRegion`'s block-light BFS runs from the lava after the sky's. **Rulings P43's fallback applies**: the scan (seed '42', profile default, T on every column of the 128 × 128 chunks x, z in −64 … 63) found no 18 × 18 region whose 256 interior columns all hold lava in their 3 × 3. By the columns' proto lava (which bounds the light region's from above, but for D's flowing lava) the most was 240; 20.0 % of the columns hold lava and 62.7 % of the 3 × 3s. A second scan of 128 × 128 chunks around the SP6b biome region (centre (−96, 96)) found none either (most 247). So the row takes the 256 lava columns from the smallest scanned area that holds them: a 17 × 17 square, the first such (the most such columns, then the lowest z, then x) at chunk (37, −43), pinned in the bench as `LAVA_AREA`. It is generated untimed on a store of its own (T on the area grown by two rings, D on it grown by one, so every column's 3 × 3 is at Decorated). Its light regions hold lava for 260 of its 289 columns (lava voxels per region p50 387, min 1, max 3,033), and the row takes the first 256 in (z, x) order, each checked on its copy in the bench.
- **`mesh.section`** (gate 1.0 / 2.5): its sample gains the non-trivial sections of the 6 × 6 interiors of `SETTLE_WINDOWS[0]` (the coast S(19, −30): spills, a water spring's falls, a flooded cave's border) and `SETTLE_WINDOWS[1]` (the water/lava contact S(1, 0) with its flowing lava; rulings P42), each window's 10 × 10 generated untimed on a store of its own (T on it grown by one ring, D on it, L on its 8 × 8). That adds 829 sections, 64 holding flowing fluid and 35 lava, after the region's 1,024 (1,853 in all, each timed once; `mesh.xray` times the pass alone on the same sample). It is read with Task 34's Lever 1 in (water top faces carry their depth band, 92997d3), whose keep-or-revert decision is pending with the user.
- **`slice.caves128`** re-measured with Task 41's aquifer arrays: 243.9-247.3 ms p50 (gate 300; ratio ×1.160 against Task 32's record).

The protocol (rulings R6, R21 item 1, R22): the EC check, then `BENCH_EXEMPT=mesh.section,mesh.xray,slice.caves128 npm run bench` (the rows whose code or sample changed since the record: `mesh.xray`'s pass changed in Task 40; `slice.biome3d128` is not exempt, since Task 41's `fluidAt` runs only in the `caves` field), then `bench:record`, then gated runs until three consecutive passes. Each run used the heavy-command lock and the 6 GB cap, with the 1-minute load below 1 at its start. EC check at 06:25, AC online: 6 own busy threads at 4.10 GHz at every sample (≤ 66 °C), 20 threads at 3.77-3.78 GHz, then 2.92 GHz at 14 s (PL1, 58 °C), `package_throttle_count` unchanged; no cap.
